# TODO — Qwen 3.5/3.6 Native Metal Port

**Goal:** Native Metal inference for Qwen 3.5 9B and 3.6 27B (arch=qwen35), targeting ≥10% faster than llama.cpp HEAD on decode, with cosine≈1.0 correctness.

**Target model:** `Qwen3.5-9B-Q4_K_M.gguf` (primary debug target) → scale to `Qwen3.6-27B-Q4_K_M.gguf`.

**llama.cpp baseline source:** `~/SrcArchives/AI/llama.cpp` (HEAD).

**Protocol:** v13.2. Quadrumvirate on each phase start. LOGBOOK in conversation, persistent state in LANDMARKS.md.

**Current harness integration frontier (2026-05-21):**
- [x] Add Qwen XML-to-JSON function-calling bridge for CrystalBall-style harnesses. The model still receives Qwen-native XML tool instructions, but host output can now be normalized as `{"content":...,"tool_calls":[{"name":...,"arguments":{...}}]}` via `QWEN35_TOOL_RESPONSE_JSON=simple`, or OpenAI wrappers via `openai`.
  - Evidence: `crystal spec spec/qwen35_chat_spec.cr spec/qwen35_tokenizer_spec.cr --error-trace` passed (`10 examples, 0 failures`); `crystal build --no-codegen bin/qwen35_generate.cr --error-trace` passed; `crystal build --no-codegen bin/qwen35_tool_json_adapter.cr --error-trace` passed; adapter parse/render smokes passed.
  - Boundary: This is not constrained JSON decoding inside the model. It is a boundary adapter: OpenAI/CrystalBall messages/tools render into Qwen chat/XML, generated Qwen XML tool calls normalize back to harness JSON.
- [x] Add the CrystalBall-side provider adapter in `../crystal_ball`. `CrystalBall::LLM::CogniQwen` shells to a built `qwen35_generate` with `QWEN35_MESSAGES_JSON`, `QWEN35_TOOLS_JSON`, and `QWEN35_TOOL_RESPONSE_JSON=simple`, then parses the final `=== Tool response JSON ===` block into `CrystalBall::LLM::ToolResponse`.
  - Evidence: `crystal build --no-codegen src/crystal_ball.cr --error-trace` passed in `../crystal_ball`; a fake `qwen35_generate` process smoke returned one parsed `read_file` tool call. Full `src/cli.cr` compile is currently blocked by an unrelated dirty `state_persistence.cr` type error in `../crystal_ball`.

- [ ] Run apples-vs-apples native/plain Q4_K_M and MTP rows after choosing quiet-host settings and rebuilding any ignored benchmark artifacts as needed.
- [ ] Decide the first native response branch after measurement: either add native IQ4_NL/UD quant support, repack MTP heads into a Q4_K_M-compatible sidecar path, or improve the existing MTP-on-self-top2-miss controller if baseline economics favor controller work over quant work.

**Current LTP/WBA speed frontier (2026-05-20):**
- [x] Add `scripts/qwen35_profile_atlas.cr`, a profile-log atlas that ranks Metal wait buckets, grouped command-buffer waits, logical matmul traffic, conversion traffic, and candidate LTP/WBA windows without claiming a speedup.

  - Current atlas signal: pp64 prepared profile reports `grouped_wait_ms=146.31`, logical traffic `3992.54 MiB` matmul + `303.00 MiB` conversion, with dominant scope `prefill.rec.ffn_upgate q4_h16_gemm Q4_K 4096x12288 b64` at `1296.00 MiB` / `32.5%` of matmul traffic. Conversion is only `7.05%` of logical traffic in this pp64 profile, so the next exact Metal prefill attempt should target repeated recurrent FFN up/gate work before another conversion-only branch.
- [ ] Use the atlas on an apples-vs-apples quiet-host pp64/pp256/pp1024 run and compare against llama.cpp profile/throughput before changing kernels. Required output: top weighted windows, paired native wall timing, llama.cpp baseline command, and one falsifier for the chosen branch.
  - [x] Relaxed-host first pass (not publishable quiet-host evidence): `/tmp/benchmark_qwen_vs_llama_atlas --native-prefill-prepare-state --prompt=64/256/1024 --gen=64 --reps=2-3 --warmup=1 --load-warning-threshold=0 --load-total-warning-threshold=0` showed prepared prefill gaps of `+4.96%` at pp64, `-7.73%` at pp256, `+6.54%` at pp1024; native top1 decode was ahead by `+3.82%/+4.24%/+24.40%` in the same runs. A first-run pp64 check without prepared state was still behind (`383.65 tok/s` native p50 vs `425.91 tok/s` llama avg, `-9.92%`).
  - [x] Atlas first pass on pp64/pp256/pp1024 prepared phase profiles: dominant matmul scope stays `prefill.rec.ffn_upgate q4_h16_gemm Q4_K 4096x12288`, but conversion traffic grows from `7.05%` at pp64 to `23.3%` at pp256 and `54.8%` at pp1024. This points to a size-dependent WBA branch: keep the short-prompt focus on recurrent FFN up/gate repeated-weight work, but investigate long-prompt activation-staging corridors for pp256+.
  - [x] Add default-off `QWEN35_ADDNORM_H16_FFN=1` probe for the long-prompt activation-staging corridor. It routes residual add+RMSNorm directly into H16 rows when the following FFN gate/up pair uses Q4_H16 GEMM. Focused specs passed (`18 examples, 0 failures`). It removes the FFN-upgate pair conversion rows at pp1024 (`conversion_mib 4848 -> 4104`) but is not a promotion candidate yet: pp256 A/B regressed (`default p50 602.05ms`, h16 `616.73ms`, wins `3/6`), while pp1024 was mixed/small-positive (`default p50 2293.69ms`, h16 `2252.83ms`, wins `1/3`).
  - [x] Add default-off `QWEN35_SWIGLU_H16_DOWN=1` probe for the FFN-down side of the same activation-staging corridor. It writes SwiGLU activations directly as H16 for FFN-down Q4/Q5/Q6 batch GEMM consumers. Build/spec gates passed (`18 examples, 0 failures`). pp1024 atlas confirms the byte move is real (`conversion_mib 4848 -> 2616`), but paired relaxed wall timing is mixed: pp256 was noise-level positive (`default p50 478.85ms`, h16 `478.59ms`), pp1024 small positive (`1915.77ms -> 1905.05ms`), and pp2048 regressed (`4424.79ms -> 4544.97ms`). Keep default-off; this is evidence that conversion removal alone is not enough once memory scheduling shifts.
  - [x] Check the combined activation-staging corridor without new code: `QWEN35_ADDNORM_H16_FFN=1` plus `QWEN35_SWIGLU_H16_DOWN=1` drops pp1024 conversion traffic further to `1872 MiB` and improves addnorm-only paired pp1024 slightly (`1908.55ms -> 1900.71ms` p50), but this still lacks direct quiet-host default-vs-combo proof and leaves `prefill.rec.o_proj`, `prefill.rec.proj`, and `prefill.full.qkv` conversions as the remaining conversion rows. Treat as a dataflow clue, not a default.
  - [x] Add default-off `QWEN35_RMSNORM_H16_PROJ=1` probe for projection-side staging. It uses dual-output RMSNorm (`f32` plus H16) for projection consumers that already cast to H16 internally. Build/spec gates passed (`18 examples, 0 failures`). pp1024 conversion traffic drops from `4848 MiB` to `3768 MiB` by removing recurrent projection shared-input and full-QKV conversions; stacking all three H16 staging knobs drops conversion to `792 MiB`, leaving mostly o_proj conversions. Wall timing is still noise-level: standalone pp256/pp1024 paired deltas were about `+0.88/+0.90ms`, and all-three pp1024 was mixed. This refutes pure conversion-byte removal as the current breakthrough lever.
  - [x] Refute default-off dual-output Q4_H16 gate/up GEMM as the repeated FFN up/gate fix. A temporary `QWEN35_Q4K_PAIR_H16_DUAL=1` kernel shared the H16 input tile and one dispatch for the two Q4_K FFN gate/up matrices, but register/shmem pressure outweighed the saved input work: pp64 regressed slightly (`145.27ms -> 145.73ms` p50), pp256 regressed strongly (`479.74ms -> 493.09ms`, default wins `6/6`), and pp1024 regressed (`1912.05ms -> 1975.05ms`, default wins `3/3`). The code branch was removed; keep the refutation as a guard against retesting this shape blindly.
  - [x] Add default-off `QWEN35_DN_POST_H16_OPROJ=1` probe for recurrent o_proj staging. It writes DeltaNet post-gate output directly as H16 for following o_proj GEMMs. Build/spec gates passed (`18 examples, 0 failures`). Standalone pp1024 conversion traffic drops `4848 -> 4272 MiB`; combined with the other H16 staging knobs it drops to `216 MiB`, leaving only full-attention o_proj and two small unscoped rows. Paired wall timing remains neutral (`pp256 -0.12ms`, `pp1024 +0.17ms`), so conversion traffic is now falsified as the limiting wall bottleneck.
  - [x] Promote exact wide-batch Q4_K H16 prefill GEMM for 64-multiple prompt chunks (`QWEN35_Q4K_H16_B64_OFF=1` disables). The legal WBA move changes the Q4_H16 batch tile from 64x32 to 64x64 with 8 simdgroups, so one dequantized weight tile feeds twice as many prompt rows. Focused specs passed (`18 examples, 0 failures`). Relaxed prepared A/B against OFF: pp64 `141.12ms` vs `143.27ms` (default wins `6/6`), pp256 `469.08ms` vs `479.32ms` (wins `6/6`), pp1024 earlier opt-in `1862.91ms` vs `1920.03ms`; pp80 is neutral because non-64-multiple chunks are intentionally routed to the old tile after an adversary check found the wide tail path regressed.
  - [x] Refresh native-vs-llama after Q4_B64 on the current relaxed host: pp64 prepared prefill improved to `451.17 tok/s` p50 vs llama `461.70 tok/s` (`-2.28%`, previously about `-3.63%` in the same session); pp256 improved to `545.66 tok/s` p50 vs llama `575.80 tok/s` (`-5.23%`, previously about `-7.64%`). Decode remains ahead by about `+3.6%..+3.8%`. This is a real exact prefill gain, but not yet a llama.cpp prefill overtake at pp64/pp256.
  - [x] Refute the direct Q5/Q6 B64 analog as a promotion candidate. A temporary opt-in `QWEN35_Q56K_H16_B64=1` branch added 64-row Q5/Q6 f32-output prefill GEMM tiles, but the relaxed paired A/B signal was too small and shape-mixed: pp64 regressed (`344.45ms` default vs `348.87ms` opt-in), pp256 longer rerun was only noise-level positive (`469.05ms` vs `466.86ms`, about `0.47%`), pp512 was similarly small (`909.98ms` vs `905.57ms`, about `0.48%`), and pp80 showed comparable noise while the modulo gate should make the route inactive. The code branch was removed. Future Q5/Q6 work needs a different schedule or shape-specific route, not a direct Q4_B64 copy.
  - [x] Refute the cheapest Q4 irregular-tail policy. A temporary `QWEN35_Q4K_H16_B64_TAIL32=1` gate allowed the existing B64 kernel on `64n+32` chunks without split-tail dispatches, but pp96 regressed hard (`401.49ms` default vs `453.48ms` opt-in, default wins `6/6`) and pp160 also regressed (`421.16ms` vs `446.72ms`, default wins `5/6`). The branch was removed. Future irregular-tail work needs a distinct low-waste tail kernel or row-packing scheme, not the 256-thread B64 kernel on half-full tiles.
  - [x] Refute Q4 split-tail32 dispatch. A temporary `QWEN35_Q4K_H16_B64_SPLIT_TAIL32=1` route ran the full 64-row prefix through B64 and the 32-row tail through the old B32 kernel with buffer offsets. It still regressed pp96 (`394.10ms` default vs `436.36ms`, default wins `6/6`) and pp160 (`416.18ms` vs `422.44ms`, default wins `5/6`). The branch was removed. Extra dispatch/encoder work outweighs the saved tile groups for current prefill shapes.
  - [x] Promote exact Q4_K H16 B96 prefill tile for exact 96-token chunks (`QWEN35_Q4K_H16_B96_OFF=1` disables). This is the first working one-dispatch irregular-tail row-packing move: one 12-simdgroup tile lets a dequantized Q4 weight tile feed 96 prompt rows. Focused specs with the default route passed (`18 examples, 0 failures`). Relaxed pp96 A/B before default promotion showed a stable median win: first repeat `203.52ms -> 201.23ms` with opt-in winning `10/10`; final `reps=20` had p50 `203.35ms -> 201.25ms` and opt-in winning `19/20`, with one outlier hurting average. After default promotion, pp96 against `QWEN35_Q4K_H16_B96_OFF=1` measured `200.86ms` vs `202.96ms`, default wins `12/12`. Wider B96 use is refuted for now: pp160 and pp192 regressed, so the default gate is exact `batch == 96` only.
  - [x] Refute exact Q4_K H16 B128 prefill tile. A temporary opt-in `QWEN35_Q4K_H16_B128=1` 16-simdgroup tile built and ran, but pp128 regressed immediately (`253.26ms` default B64 route vs `269.43ms` opt-in, default wins `3/3`). The code branch was removed. The likely cause is occupancy/resource pressure from 512-thread groups and 32 KiB threadgroup memory outweighing one fewer Q4 dequant pass.
  - [x] Promote exact Q4_K H16 B80 prefill tile for exact 80-token chunks (`QWEN35_Q4K_H16_B80_OFF=1` disables). The 10-simdgroup B80 tile is a stronger row-packing win than B96 on this host: opt-in pp80 measured `204.26ms -> 191.09ms`, wins `10/10` (`~6.9%` p50). After default promotion, pp80 against `QWEN35_Q4K_H16_B80_OFF=1` measured `191.07ms` vs `203.73ms`, default wins `12/12`. Focused specs with the default route passed (`18 examples, 0 failures`). Neighbor guard prompts were route-inactive/noise-level (`pp64`, `pp96`, `pp112`). This validates the row-packing direction below the B128 resource cliff.
  - [x] Promote exact Q4_K H16 B48 prefill tile for exact 48-token chunks (`QWEN35_Q4K_H16_B48_OFF=1` disables). The 6-simdgroup B48 tile is a small but consistent row-packing win over the old B32+B16-equivalent route on this host. Opt-in pp48 evidence was noisy in short runs, then stabilized at `147.30ms -> 146.03ms` p50 and `147.70ms -> 146.19ms` average over `40` paired reps, with opt-in winning `34/40`. After default promotion, pp48 against `QWEN35_Q4K_H16_B48_OFF=1` measured `143.77ms` vs `144.47ms` p50, default wins `31/40`. Focused specs with the default route passed (`18 examples, 0 failures`). This is a lower-ROI but low-risk exact gate because it activates only for `batch == 48`.
  - [x] Promote exact Q4_K H16 B112 prefill tile for exact 112-token chunks (`QWEN35_Q4K_H16_B112_OFF=1` disables). The 14-simdgroup B112 tile stays below the B128 resource cliff and gives a strong row-packing win: opt-in pp112 first pass measured `270.85ms -> 251.99ms`, wins `8/8`; longer opt-in measured `268.98ms -> 253.23ms`, wins `19/20`. After default promotion, pp112 against `QWEN35_Q4K_H16_B112_OFF=1` measured `252.75ms` vs `270.10ms` p50, default wins `20/20`. Focused specs with the default route passed (`18 examples, 0 failures`). This refines the cliff model: 448-thread/28 KiB exact-shape tiles can still win, while B128's 512-thread/32 KiB tile regressed.
  - [x] Refute graph-level Q4 split160 via two B80 sub-dispatches. A temporary opt-in `QWEN35_Q4K_H16_SPLIT160=1` route split exact pp160 into two offset B80 GEMM calls. It built and ran, but regressed strongly: pp160 measured `303.48ms` default vs `316.55ms` split, default wins `10/10`. Extra dispatch/offset scheduling outweighed the saved B80 row packing for this shape. The code branch was removed.
  - [x] Refresh matched native-vs-llama after B48/B80/B96/B112 row-packing. Relaxed prepared-state `prompt/gen=64` results: pp64 native `448.82 tok/s` vs llama `464.23` (`-3.32%`), pp80 `424.61` vs `426.55` (`-0.45%`), pp96 `479.06` vs `505.07` (`-5.15%`), pp112 `469.86` vs `458.68` (`+2.44%`), pp256 `541.92` vs `576.69` (`-6.03%`). Decode remains ahead by `+3.24..+5.08%`. Conclusion: exact-shape Q4 row-packing moved selected shapes to parity/win, but pp256 remains a broader scheduling/operator gap.
  - [x] Revalidate combined H16 activation staging after row-packing. On pp256, enabling `QWEN35_ADDNORM_H16_FFN=1 QWEN35_SWIGLU_H16_DOWN=1 QWEN35_RMSNORM_H16_PROJ=1 QWEN35_DN_POST_H16_OPROJ=1` reduced conversion logical traffic from `1212 MiB` to `54 MiB`, but wall stayed nearly flat (`469.50ms` default attribution p50 vs `467.41ms` staged p50, unpaired). This confirms the older conclusion under the new baseline: conversion is visible, but not the primary pp256 wall bottleneck.
  - [x] Refute Q5/Q6 direct f32out store. A temporary opt-in `QWEN35_Q56K_DIRECT_F32OUT=1` skipped the current `float(half(acc))` staging path and wrote exact-tile simdgroup accumulators directly to f32 output. Focused specs still passed, but the route is semantically risky and not faster: pp256 measured `476.40ms` default vs `477.04ms` opt-in p50, wins `5/10`. The probe code was removed.
  - [x] Add default-off Q4 B64 up-projection + SwiGLU fusion probe (`QWEN35_Q4K_B64_UP_SWIGLU=1`). This exact producer-consumer WBA move replaces `up GEMM -> swiglu kernel` with a B64 up GEMM that reads the already materialized gate projection and writes `silu(gate) * up` directly to the FFN activation buffer. It stays opt-in because it trades the B64 direct device store for a fused cooperative write. Build/spec gates pass (`18 examples, 0 failures`). Relaxed prepared A/B is small-positive but not a default-on proof: pp256 `469.45ms -> 468.56ms` p50 with opt-in winning `15/20`, pp1024 `1919.63ms -> 1914.68ms` p50 with opt-in winning `4/5`, pp64 neutral (`141.49ms -> 141.27ms`, `6/10`). Keep as a controlled exact probe and candidate fusion direction, not public benchmark evidence.
  - [x] Verify native prefill benchmark semantics before changing the route. A temporary pp256 diagnostic showed `prefill_tokens_top1` is not adding a large final-head tax versus body-only prompt ingestion; it is slightly faster because it uses the optimized final-layer last-row route. Repeated local M2 Max run measured body-only p50 `499.50ms` (`512.51 tok/s`) vs top1 p50 `491.59ms` (`520.76 tok/s`). Therefore the remaining pp256 gap is a real scheduling/operator gap, not a benchmark artifact from using `prefill_tokens_top1`.
  - [x] Promote exact Q4 B64 up-projection + H16 SwiGLU fusion for 64-multiple prefill chunks (`QWEN35_Q4K_B64_UP_SWIGLU_H16_OFF=1` disables). This is the stronger producer-consumer diamond: the up B64 kernel reads the materialized gate projection and writes `half(silu(gate) * up)` directly for the downstream H16 FFN-down route, eliminating both the standalone SwiGLU kernel and the FFN-down input conversion for Q4/Q6 consumers. Focused default specs passed (`18 examples, 0 failures`). Relaxed pp256 A/B against OFF measured default p50 `603.21ms` vs OFF `609.29ms`, default wins `8/10`; an earlier opt-in run before default promotion measured `493.38ms -> 489.40ms`, wins `10/10`. Conversion traffic at pp256 drops from `1212 MiB` to `654 MiB`. Non-64 guard pp80 remains route-inactive and within host noise.
  - [x] Revalidate the remaining H16 staging knobs after Q4 B64 H16-SwiGLU promotion. `QWEN35_ADDNORM_H16_FFN=1` now removes the fused route's remaining `q4_pair_swiglu_h16_input` conversion and is small-positive but weak at pp256 (`504.27ms -> 503.62ms`, opt-in wins `6/8`). Stacking `ADDNORM_H16_FFN=1 QWEN35_RMSNORM_H16_PROJ=1 QWEN35_DN_POST_H16_OPROJ=1` drops conversion traffic to `54 MiB`, but wall stays flat/slightly worse (`488.19ms -> 488.71ms` when toggling RMS on top of addnorm+dn-post). Conclusion: after the promoted producer-consumer fusion, remaining conversion staging is not a primary breakthrough lever.
  - [ ] Rerun the same comparison under a quiet-host gate before changing public benchmark docs. Current relaxed evidence is enough for branch selection, not for final claims.
  - [ ] Next exact branch: use the B48/B80/B96/B112 result as the positive row-packing pattern, but avoid larger monolithic tiles and extra-dispatch splitting. Explore exact `batch == 144` only if the tile can avoid the B128 512-thread/32KiB cliff, or shift to in-kernel row packing / graph-level batching that preserves B48/B64/B80/B96/B112 weight reuse without new dispatches. Avoid dual-output Q4 accumulator doubling, direct Q5/Q6 B64 copying, B64-on-tail32, split-tail32 dispatch, B128, and split160; all six branches are refuted.

## Phase 1a — Q4_K CPU path

- [x] **1a.1** `Dequant.dequantize_q4_k` in `src/ml/gguf/dequant.cr` (block: d/dmin/scales[12]/qs[128] = 144 B)
- [x] **1a.2** `QuantMatmul.matmul_add_q4k` in `src/ml/gguf/quant_matmul.cr`

## Phase 1b — CPU-reference Qwen 3.5 9B forward

  - [x] Reuse Q4_K simdgroup-matrix GEMM inside GPU-resident recurrent prefill chunks; pp64 p50 improves from `56.70 tok/s` to `70.38 tok/s`
  - [x] Chunk full-attention prefill layers on Metal; pp64 p50 improves from `70.38 tok/s` to `143.18 tok/s`
  - [x] Enable Q5_K/Q6_K batch GEMM in prefill chunks with GPU-side F32↔F16 conversion; pp64 p50 improves from `143.18 tok/s` to `278.50 tok/s`
  - [x] Keep DeltaNet chunk state rows register-resident across the token scan; pp64 p50 improves from `278.50 tok/s` to `308.86 tok/s`
  - [x] Keep consecutive recurrent prefill layers GPU-resident; pp64 p50 improves from `308.86 tok/s` to `327.54 tok/s`
  - [x] Batch the final prompt token into prefill and run only fused lm-head top1; pp64 p50 improves from `308.82 tok/s` to `358.44 tok/s`
  - [x] Fast-path full Q4_K GEMM output tiles with direct simdgroup stores; pp64 p50 improves from `358.44 tok/s` to `373.80 tok/s`

  - [x] Add `--prepare-state` to the prefill attribution harness so kernel/profile work can measure prepared-state prompt ingest separately from lazy first-touch state allocation; pp64 prepared profile measured `142.31 ms` p50 / `449.72 tok/s` with the same hot-shape mix.
  - [x] In-place SwiGLU staging for chunked prefill FFN paths; exact aliasing path passes specs and small pp64 A/B improves default by `~0.48 ms` vs `QWEN35_SWIGLU_INPLACE_OFF=1`

The CLI now prepares target and neural-speculative draft/backup states before latency-sensitive prompt/decode sections, using the same `prepare_state_metal!` path measured in the benchmark harness.
  - [x] Falsifier/tuning note: initial `gamma=3` is not a safe default even though it is faster on the 100%-accept prompt (`~18.3 ms/tok` vs `~19.4`) and mixed partial-reject prompts (`Once upon a time` `21.56` vs gamma4 `22.42`), because it regresses `def fibonacci(n):` to `22.74 ms/tok`; `--max-gamma 16/24` did not recover the high-accept gain, so the effect is the initial chunk schedule, not just the cap
  - [x] Enable exact early-reject and gamma=1 accepted-token fast paths for `--verify serial` too: serial verifier is still not the default for high-accept prompts (`28.83 ms/tok` vs chunk `19.34`), but partial-reject profiling now avoids artificial draft/span waste (`Once upon a time` serial-fast `21.77` vs chunk `22.31`; `The quick brown fox` `21.77` vs chunk `22.41`)
  - [x] Add opt-in `--verify hybrid` mode: first speculative cycle uses serial verifier, later cycles use chunk-inplace. This helps first-cycle partial-reject prompts (`Once upon a time` `21.44` vs chunk `22.47`; `The quick brown fox` `21.73` vs `22.49`) but is not default because high-accept prompt slightly regresses (`19.32` vs `19.08`) and `def fibonacci(n):` regresses (`21.68` vs `21.50`)
  - [x] Falsifier: a progressive verifier that serial-probes 1/2/3 first-cycle candidates and chunks the rest preserved exactness, but it is not worth carrying: high-accept regressed (`19.41-19.70 ms/tok` vs chunk `19.04`), partial-reject prompts were weaker than `hybrid`, and `def fibonacci(n):` regressed (`21.67-21.90` vs chunk `21.51`); temporary code was removed
  - [x] Falsifier: Q8_0 mixed-size dual GEMV for draft recurrent `qkv + gate` lowers `rec.proj` encode accounting (`~3.00 ms` -> `~2.36-2.42 ms` over 16 tokens), but worsens end-to-end draft decode (`~7.85-7.88 ms/tok` separate GEMVs vs `~8.05-8.06 ms/tok` mixed), so this is another dispatch-saving/register-pressure tradeoff rather than a speed path
  - [x] Reuse the existing Q8_0 dual GEMV kernel for draft recurrent `alpha + beta` projections (`QWEN35_Q8_ALPHA_BETA_DUAL_GEMV_OFF=1` disables it): 0.8B draft sync profile improves from `8.77/8.90 ms/tok` off to `8.51/8.43 ms/tok` default over 64 tokens, while focused Qwen specs stay green
  - [x] Skip speculative draft-state backup when a future rejection is guaranteed to enter target-only fallback (`QWEN35_SPEC_SKIP_DRAFT_BACKUP_BEFORE_FALLBACK_OFF=1` disables): exact harness output remains matched to greedy target, and partial-reject prompts avoid the unused draft copy (`draft_backup_skip=1`, `draft_backup=0.0 ms`)
  - [x] Add guarded fast regrowth after adaptive gamma reaches `8` (`QWEN35_SPEC_FAST_REGROW_MIN_GAMMA=0` disables): high-accept prompt now reaches `gamma=32` in `5` cycles instead of `7`, improving `The capital of France is` from `~19.2 ms/tok` to `~16.0-17.8 ms/tok`; `def fibonacci(n):` stays on the safe path (`max_seen=4`, fallback after reject)
  - [x] Reuse the existing Q8_0 dual GEMV kernel for full-attention `K + V` projections (`QWEN35_Q8_KV_DUAL_GEMV_OFF=1` disables): draft full.qkv encode drops from `~3.7 ms` to `~2.7 ms` over 64 tokens and high-accept speculative decode gets a small exact win (`15.73 ms/tok` off -> `15.68/15.71 ms/tok` default)
  - [x] Falsifier: switching fallback-bound low-gamma verifier cycles from `chunk-inplace` to forked `chunk` removes target backup but is not a win; high-accept regresses (`~15.8` -> `~16.0 ms/tok`) and reject prompts are neutral, so keep `chunk-inplace` default
  - [x] Falsifier: raising initial speculative `gamma` from `4` to `5` or `6` is worse under the current fast-regrow schedule; high-accept prompts slow slightly, `def fibonacci(n):` regresses (`gamma=5` verifies one extra reject row, `gamma=6` breaks into many low-gamma cycles), and partial-reject prompts lose acceptance efficiency
  - [x] Falsifier: enabling batched lm-head top1 only for large verifier chunks (`>=8/16/24/32` rows) is still slower than per-row fused top1; high-accept target verifier rises from `~552-556 ms` to `~627-675 ms`, so leave `QWEN35_HEAD_TOP1_ROWS` default-off
  - [x] Refresh: lowering Q4_K pair H16 prefill threshold to b64 is still not a robust pp64 win; it halves FFN upgate conversion accounting but wall remains neutral/noisy (`~156.3-156.6 ms` default vs `~156.1-156.3 ms` branch under host load), so keep pair route gated at batch `>=256`
  - [x] CUDA raw-Q8 controller infrastructure: expose full-attention KV device buffers and add `QwenMixedStackRunner#copy_decode_state_to!` for recurrent conv/SSM plus K/V cache device-to-device copies. Local no-codegen and remote RTX 5060 Ti probe build/run pass; speed-bearing chunk verifier remains pending, and duplicate verifier stacks are flagged as a memory-risk architecture.
  - [x] CUDA same-stack raw-Q8 rollback: add `snapshot_decode_state` / `restore_decode_state` with optional KV coverage and route `--greedy-loop-probe-restore` through it. All-layer Qwen3.5-9B remote smokes preserve exact greedy output for recurrent-only rollback and `--greedy-loop-probe-restore-kv`; keep KV rollback as an adversary flag, not the speed default.
  - [x] CUDA raw-Q8 guarded chunk diagnostic: add `--greedy-loop-probe-chunk-gamma=N` plus margin fallback. Remote all-layer Qwen3.5-9B gen16 micro-suite over seeds `0,198,760,1919` preserved exact output; gamma2 full accepts `8/9,8/8,8/8,8/8`, gamma4 full accepts `4/5,4/4,4/4,4/4`, with one seed0 near-tie margin fallback. This is controller accounting only; verifier remains sequential.
  - [x] Falsifier: duplicate-stack `--greedy-loop-probe-chunk-batched-verify` preserves exact output after switching verifier truth to materialized-logits scan, but it is not a speed path. Remote gamma2 verifier chunks cost `~46.2-46.4 ms/chunk` and gamma4 `~91.9-92.0 ms/chunk`, still `~23 ms/token`; total diagnostic wall remains `~49.7-51.6 ms/tok` because raw-Q8 proposal is `~21.8 ms/token` and verifier work is not sublinear.
  - [x] Falsifier: switching Q5_K batch GEMM from the current double-buffered schedule to a llama-style single-buffer schedule is exact but slower on pp64; focused specs pass (`22 examples, 0 failures`), but paired A/B loses (`159.99 ms` default vs `161.55 ms` single-buffer p50, default wins `7/8`), so keep Q5 double-buffering
  - [x] Falsifier: skipping full-attention KV copies in speculative target rollback is exact but not faster; focused rollback spec passed, yet high-accept smoke stayed neutral/slightly worse (`16.30 ms/tok` skip-KV vs `16.20 ms/tok` full backup) and `target_backup` remained `10.6 ms`, showing rollback cost is recurrent state buffers rather than KV
  - [x] Falsifier: Q8_0 GEMV shared-X threadgroup staging is exact but slower for the 0.8B draft; the focused Q8 GEMV spec worsened (`~1.9 ms` -> `24.3 ms`) and draft decode regressed from `8.26` to `9.18 ms/tok`, so current Q8 speed is not limited by repeated x loads
  - [x] Falsifier: Q8_0 block-major weight prepack is not a material GEMV speed path for the 0.8B draft on M2 Max. A real-weight Metal microbench compared the current row-major GGUF block layout with `[block][row]` packed layout and stayed exact (`max_delta=0`), but measured only `~1.009x` on `blk.0.ffn_up 1024x3584`, `~0.999x` on `blk.1.attn_qkv 1024x6144`, and `~1.002x` on `blk.0.ffn_down 3584x1024`; do not add load-time Q8 prepacking unless a different kernel design changes the access pattern.
  - [x] Falsifier: making speculative gamma regrow more aggressive is not a safe default. `QWEN35_SPEC_FULL_ACCEPT_STREAK=1`, `QWEN35_SPEC_FAST_REGROW_MIN_GAMMA=4`, and initial `--gamma 8` all stayed neutral/slower on the 100%-accept prompt (`~16.4-16.9 ms/tok` vs default `16.44`) and regressed `def fibonacci(n):` (`24.75-24.97 ms/tok` vs default `22.98`), so keep the conservative current schedule
  - [x] Add opt-in staged speculative verifier mode for high-accept prompts (`--verify staged --stage-gate 4`). It drafts/verifies a guard stage before the rest of a large gamma cycle and preserves exact greedy output; with fixed `--gamma 32`, high-accept `The capital of France is` improves from default `16.45` to `15.04 ms/tok`, but rejection-sensitive `def fibonacci(n):` regresses to `32.58 ms/tok`, so the mode stays opt-in and not default
  - [x] Falsifier: Q6 lm-head top1 tile rows `12 -> 16` is correctness-safe but not a verifier win; focused specs passed (`13 examples, 0 failures`), yet high-accept target verifier stayed flat (`~573.8 ms` default, `~478-480 ms` staged) and reject default worsened (`22.84 -> 23.45 ms/tok`), so keep rows12
  - [x] Falsifier/fix: full-row batched Q6 lm-head verifier is fast but not exact because its F16 GEMM path can flip close argmax rows (`row24`: exact `220@8.900501` vs F16 full-row `198@8.8984375`). Keep it opt-in with `QWEN35_HEAD_FULL_ROWS=1`; default verifier returned to exact per-row fused top1 and a rows32 ambiguous regression spec now covers this case
  - [x] Fix external speculative verifier fail-closed around guarded full-row target chunks: `qwen35_speculative_accept` now disables `QWEN35_HEAD_FULL_ROWS_GUARDED` inside neural verifier/correction chunks unless `--allow-guarded-verifier` / `QWEN35_SPEC_ALLOW_GUARDED_VERIFIER=1` is explicit. Unsafe guarded mode matched llama.cpp on a high-repeat France prompt (`11.56 ms/tok`, `86.49 tok/s`, `100%` accept) but diverged under `bootstrap32`; the safe exact fix restores parity and a 3-prompt sweep completes without failures (`default` avg `1.153x`, `bootstrap32` avg `1.169x`, with bootstrap bad on code).
  - [x] Port the guarded-verifier fail-closed rule into `qwen35_generate` speculative chunks too; CLI neural speculative verification now disables `QWEN35_HEAD_FULL_ROWS_GUARDED` around chunk verification/correction, matching the exact probe behavior.
  - [x] Add exact `--bootstrap-streak` / `QWEN35_SPEC_BOOTSTRAP_STREAK` policy control, `bootstrap32_s2` sweep preset, and the same env knob in `qwen35_generate`. Requiring two full-accept chunks before jumping to gamma32 avoids the code-prompt collapse from one-shot `bootstrap32`; the 3-prompt gate wins `3/3` vs default (`19.02 ms/tok` avg vs `19.64`, paired ratio `0.962x`), and a broader reps=2 8-prompt gate wins `9/16` with paired ratio `0.979x`.
  - [x] Add `ngram_bootstrap32_s2` sweep preset after exact n-gram front-running proved to be the strongest current policy. On an 8-prompt mix, `--ngram` alone improved paired ratio to `0.853x` vs default, and `ngram + bootstrap32_s2` stayed strong at `0.873x`; France reaches `~10.1-11.3 ms/tok`, faster than the local llama.cpp speculative run (`~11.75 ms/tok`) on the same target/draft pair.
  - [x] Add exact Q5_K/Q6_K prefill GEMM f32-output kernels that preserve the previous half-rounded output contract but skip the separate half-output → f32 conversion dispatch; also vectorize Q4_K H16 input tile loads. Focused specs pass (`23 examples, 0 failures`), standalone pp64 op attribution drops measured weighted hot-shape lower bound from `8.209 ms` to `2.913 ms`, while full pp64/pp256 wall remains host/noise limited around `153 ms` / `494 ms`
  - [x] Add matched benchmark mode for preallocated prompt state (`--native-prefill-prealloc`): it allocates GPU state once, resets only recurrent conv/SSM state between reps, and preserves exact `start_pos=0` output (`fresh=72:9.439041`, `reuse=72:9.439041`). Prompt64/gen64 smoke improves native prefill from `165.90 ms` p50 default to `156.27 ms` p50 preallocated, moving the matched gap from `-3.10%` to `-1.57%`; this is session-style state reuse/harness attribution, not a first-run kernel breakthrough.
  - [x] WBA/LTP next-frame saved: Qwen35 already uses wave scheduling, so the next breakthrough target is not "add WBA" but lift WBA from command-buffer groups to algebraic operator diamonds. Prime candidate is FFN `normed -> gate/up -> SwiGLU -> down -> residual` as a tile-streamed exact diamond, plus speculative verifier as a time-axis WBA batch.
  - [x] Falsifier: extending the existing in-place SwiGLU activation trick from prefill into decode wave is correctness-safe but not a default win. `QWEN35_DECODE_SWIGLU_INPLACE=1` passes targeted specs (`17 examples, 0 failures`) but paired decode A/B loses/noisy versus default (`25.135 ms/tok` default vs `25.786 ms/tok` opt-in; default wins `5/8`), so keep it default-off and treat full tile-streaming FFN as the real diamond, not buffer aliasing.
  - [x] Promote exact row-batched verifier top1 only for large chunks (`QWEN35_HEAD_TOP1_ROWS_MIN`, default `8`; `QWEN35_HEAD_TOP1_ROWS_OFF=1` disables): targeted specs pass (`17 examples, 0 failures`), high-accept speculative A/B wins `4/4` against rows-off (`16.29/21.04/21.13/19.90 ms/tok` auto vs `17.63/21.30/22.10/23.93 ms/tok` off), while rejection-heavy paths with `gamma=4` stay below the threshold and use the old per-row exact top1 path.
  - [x] Falsifier: making the old full-row Q6 lm-head route use f32 output/reduce still does not make it exact. Focused specs passed (`17 examples, 0 failures`), but live greedy equivalence diverged on `The capital of France is` at token 17 (`plain ... 13 ...` vs speculative `32` after the f32-output attempt), so no code was retained and `QWEN35_HEAD_FULL_ROWS=1` remains an unsafe upper-bound/research knob rather than a verifier path.
  - [x] Falsifier: adaptive staged verifier and regrow scheduling are not default wins after row-batched top1. `--verify staged --stage-gate 8/16` regresses the high-accept prompt (`18.76 ms/tok` chunk-inplace vs `26.86/21.79`) and does not help rejection-heavy prompts; `QWEN35_SPEC_ADAPTIVE_REGROW=1` reintroduces expensive draft/resync work and regresses `def fibonacci(n):` (`23.77 -> 48.00 ms/tok`).
  - [x] Falsifier: naive prefix-copy of Metal KV buffers in `State.copy_from!` is not safe with the current state-position invariant. Copying only `position / max_seq` bytes broke focused verifier specs (`chunked top1 verifier` and `target/draft constants` logit mismatches), so state copy stays full-buffer until KV valid-length is explicitly tracked per layer/buffer.
  - [x] Add opt-in speculative bootstrap jump (`--bootstrap-gamma N` / `QWEN35_SPEC_BOOTSTRAP_GAMMA`): on 100%-accept `The capital of France is`, bootstrap32 cuts cycles `5 -> 3` and improves `17.89 -> 15.10 ms/tok`; it remains default-off because `def fibonacci(n):` regresses (`23.36 -> 29.93`) after an initially accepted prefix.
  - [x] Fresh matched llama.cpp benchmark: with preallocated native prompt state, pp64 prefill beats llama.cpp by `+35.53%` (`335.25 tok/s` p50 native vs `247.37 tok/s` llama.cpp); decode is effectively parity/slightly ahead at `+1.03%` (`16.32 tok/s` p50 native vs `16.15 tok/s` llama.cpp).
  - [x] Falsifier: approximate draft layer truncation is not a useful exact speculative speed path for the current 0.8B Q8_0 draft. The draft has `24` layers, so `--draft-layers 24/26/28` is the full model; the first real truncation tested (`22`) collapses high-accept prompts to immediate reject/fallback (`The capital of France is`: `100%` full-draft accept at `~15.55 ms/tok` vs `0%` accept and `~20.63 ms/tok` with 22 layers), while partial-reject prompts also lose acceptance.
  - [x] Falsifier: lowering exact row-batched verifier top1 threshold from `8` rows to `4` is not a safe default. A noisy but adversarial four-prompt smoke showed mixed results: `def fibonacci(n):` improved in that run (`32.61 -> 30.91 ms/tok`), but high-accept `The capital of France is` regressed (`19.58 -> 22.60 ms/tok`) and `The quick brown fox` regressed (`31.08 -> 33.63 ms/tok`), so keep `QWEN35_HEAD_TOP1_ROWS_MIN=8`.
  - [x] Add exact n-gram/cache speculative harness (`bin/qwen35_ngram_speculative.cr`) as a non-neural draft paradigm. It keeps target verification exact and proposes candidates only after a repeated suffix of at least 6 tokens; high-repeat `The capital of France is` accepts `55/55` n-gram candidates and measures `17.85 ms/tok` in the smoke, while `def fibonacci(n):` proposes no candidates and falls back to plain target steps instead of burning verifier chunks on weak 1-grams.
  - [x] Extract n-gram draft selection into reusable `ML::GGUF::NgramDraft` with focused specs, so the next step can compose n-gram draft with the neural Q8_0 draft without duplicating suffix-matching logic.
  - [x] Add recursive n-gram candidate extension: once a repeated suffix is found, the draft can extend through its own scratch history up to `gamma=32` while still being target-verified exactly. High-repeat `The capital of France is` improves from non-recursive `~17.46 ms/tok` / `7` verifier cycles to recursive `~9.0 ms/tok` / `2` cycles; no-repeat `def fibonacci(n):` still proposes no candidates and falls back to plain target steps.
  - [x] Add n-gram fail-closed guard: after the first rejected n-gram chunk, the harness disables further n-gram drafting by default (`--keep-ngram-after-reject` / `QWEN35_NGRAM_DISABLE_AFTER_REJECT_OFF=1` restores exploration). This preserves the high-repeat win and avoids repeated verifier waste on partial-repeat prompts.
  - [x] Compose n-gram/cache draft with the neural speculative harness behind `--ngram` / `QWEN35_SPEC_NGRAM=1`. The n-gram front-runner is exact target-verified, lazy-resyncs the Q8_0 draft only if neural drafting is needed afterward, and improves high-repeat `The capital of France is` from neural-only `17.08 ms/tok` to n-gram+neural `11.24 ms/tok` in the same built harness while leaving no-repeat adversary prompts effectively unchanged.
  - [x] Falsifier: target-only seeding before the first n-gram match is unsafe with the current chunk verifier. It improves high-repeat `The capital of France is` to `~8.85 ms/tok`, but adversary `The quick brown fox` diverges from greedy at token 31 even when row-batched top1 is disabled for the n-gram chunk, so no target-seed code was retained.
  - [x] Add opt-in exact n-gram speculative decode to the practical generation CLI (`QWEN35_NGRAM_DECODE=1`). `qwen35_generate` now reuses `ML::GGUF::NgramDraft`, verifies chunks with the target path, fails closed after first n-gram rejection by default, and preserves exact greedy token IDs on both high-repeat `The capital of France is` (`55/55` n-gram accepted) and adversary `The quick brown fox` (`18/32` accepted then disabled).
  - [x] Add opt-in exact neural speculative decode to the practical generation CLI (`QWEN35_SPECULATIVE_DECODE=1`, optional `QWEN35_DRAFT_MODEL`). It reuses the target chunk-inplace verifier, adaptive gamma, early reject, and fallback policy; 32-token parity smokes match greedy output on high-accept and rejection prompts, while 64-token high-accept generation improves from `20.40 ms/tok` greedy to `16.61 ms/tok` neural speculative and `15.10 ms/tok` with guarded full-row verification.
  - [x] Falsifier/fix: composing n-gram and neural speculative inside `qwen35_generate` is not a speed win and exposed an exactness hazard when guarded full-row verification is applied to partial n-gram rejects. The retained fix is fail-closed: n-gram verifier chunks temporarily disable `QWEN35_HEAD_FULL_ROWS_GUARDED`, preserving greedy parity even if the env is set; ngram+neural state-machine composition was not retained.
  - [x] Apply the same fail-closed n-gram verifier guard to the research speculative harness (`bin/qwen35_speculative_accept.cr`): n-gram chunks now temporarily disable `QWEN35_HEAD_FULL_ROWS_GUARDED`, while neural chunks can still use the guard. Focused forward specs pass, high-repeat n-gram+guard smoke stays exact at `9.90 ms/tok`, and partial-repeat `The quick brown fox` stays exact with n-gram disabled after first reject.
  - [x] Fix thresholded row-batched verifier activation: `prefill_tokens_top1s` now always asks the routed output-head helper, which auto-enables exact row-batched top1 only for chunks at or above `QWEN35_HEAD_TOP1_ROWS_MIN=8` and still respects `QWEN35_HEAD_TOP1_ROWS_OFF=1`. Current smoke on `The capital of France is` improves high-accept speculative wall from `17.0 ms/tok` with rows-off to `16.43-16.67 ms/tok` default; `def fibonacci(n):` and `The quick brown fox` stay exact and remain on the gamma-4/per-row fallback path.
  - [x] Add opt-in guarded full-row verifier accelerator (`QWEN35_HEAD_FULL_ROWS_GUARDED=1`): large verifier chunks run the fast full-row Q6/F16 path, compute top1/top2 margins, and exactly fall back through row-batched fused top1 for low-margin rows. It is not default because accepted fast rows still rely on the F16 margin guard, but exact harness smokes passed; high-accept `The capital of France is` improved to `14.32 ms/tok`, and n-gram+neural improved to `9.20 ms/tok` in fresh local runs.
  - [x] Add the first exact FFN WBA sub-diamond in decode wave: Q4_K/Q6_K FFN-down GEMV can now fold the final residual add into the GEMV output write (`QWEN35_FFN_DOWN_ADD_FUSED_OFF=1` disables). Focused specs pass, default-vs-off token IDs match on `The quick brown fox`, sync profile removes `rec.add/full.add` trace kernels and improves the short smoke (`24.82 ms/tok` default vs `29.57 ms/tok` off), while paired A/B under noisy load is small/neutral-to-positive (`+0.067..0.312 ms/tok`).
  - [x] Extend the exact FFN-down residual-add WBA path to Q8_0 draft GEMV: the 0.8B draft wave now emits `rec.ffn_down_add/full.ffn_down_add` instead of `ffn_down + add`. Focused Metal/forward specs pass, and paired 0.8B draft A/B improves from `8.257 ms/tok` off to `8.102 ms/tok` default (`7/8` wins), though full speculative wall is still target-verifier dominated.
  - [x] Add an env-tunable Q4 pair-H16 prefill threshold (`QWEN35_Q4K_PAIR_H16_MIN_BATCH`). Historical default `256` avoided noisy pp64/pp128 evidence, but after later H16 routing cleanups a refreshed threshold sweep supports default `64`: threshold64 beats threshold256 at pp64 (`150.05 ms` vs `150.38 ms`, `12/12` wins in the repeat) and pp128 (`263.66 ms` vs `264.07 ms`, branch wins `5/6`); default-vs-off remains small-positive (`pp64 -0.13 ms`, `5/8`; `pp128 -0.57 ms`, `5/6`).
  - [x] Falsifier: lowering `QWEN35_Q4K_PAIR_H16_MIN_BATCH` from `64` to `32` is not a useful short-prompt win. Prepared-state A/B was neutral at pp32 (`92.42` vs `92.40 ms`, wins `2/6`) and pp64 (`142.95` vs `142.87 ms`, wins `1/6`), and slightly worse at pp128 (`256.28` vs `256.50 ms`, wins `5/6` for default), so keep the current threshold at `64`.
  - [x] Falsifier/default-off feature: ported the same FFN-down residual-add WBA pattern to prefill chunk routes for Q6_K batch GEMM (`QWEN35_PREFILL_FFN_DOWN_ADD_FUSED=1` enables). Correctness specs pass in both default and opt-in modes, but paired prefill A/B does not justify default-on: pp64 regressed in the original lazy-state run, and a refreshed prepared-state check is neutral/negative at pp64 (`142.32 ms` default vs `142.43 ms` opt-in, `4/8`) and pp256 (`477.15 ms` default vs `478.33 ms` opt-in, `4/6`). Earlier Q4 batch-add attempts were rejected because cooperative stores lost the direct simdgroup-store advantage, and an accidental Q4 batch-to-GEMV route catastrophically slowed pp64/pp256; keep the safe Q6-only path opt-in only as a controlled experiment.
  - [x] Falsifier: caching the four per-lane `DeltaNet` post-norm `Y[d]` values in registers removes a second global read in theory, but it is not a wall win in the current post kernel. Focused Metal specs pass, yet pp64 worsened (`152.56 ms` old p50 vs `155.71 ms` patched p50) and pp256 was neutral (`492.55 ms` old vs `492.23 ms` patched), so the patch was removed; the next recurrent breakthrough must fuse the DeltaNet/post boundary at the scan level or change the scan algorithm, not just local-cache the post pass.
  - [x] Add explicit practical CLI decode policy (`QWEN35_DECODE_POLICY=greedy|ngram|speculative|auto`) and fail-fast legacy conflict handling. `auto` is intentionally conservative and maps to the exact fail-closed n-gram path for now; real CLI parity checks against greedy pass for `The capital of France is`, `The quick brown fox`, and `def fibonacci(n):` at 32 generated tokens, while setting both legacy mode envs without an explicit policy now errors before model load instead of silently choosing neural speculative.
  - [x] Falsifier: extending FFN-down residual-add fusion into the `full_attn_layer_chunk_project_last` prefill-final path is correctness-safe but not a speed win. Focused Qwen/Metal specs passed (`23 examples, 0 failures`), yet pp64 paired A/B slightly favored the old separate `ffn_down + add` route (`150.64 ms` fused vs `150.45 ms` old, fused wins `3/8`), so no source change was retained.
  - [x] Reduce exact n-gram draft overhead on no-candidate paths: recursive mode now probes the current history once and only duplicates/extends scratch history after a first match exists. Focused n-gram specs pass, an isolated 2048-token no-match microbench improves slightly (`370.43 -> 358.58 ms` over 5000 calls), and real `QWEN35_DECODE_POLICY=auto` parity still matches greedy on the three 32-token CLI smoke prompts.
  - [x] Port the harness-only speculative bootstrap jump into the practical generation CLI (`QWEN35_SPEC_BOOTSTRAP_GAMMA=N`, default off). It remains opt-in because earlier harness evidence showed high-accept speedups but regressions on prompts that reject after an accepted prefix; CLI parity smokes with bootstrap32 still match greedy token IDs on high-accept and rejection prompts.
  - [x] Port exact draft-backup/resync skip before target-only fallback into `qwen35_generate` (`QWEN35_SPEC_SKIP_DRAFT_BEFORE_FALLBACK_OFF=1` and `QWEN35_SPEC_SKIP_DRAFT_BACKUP_BEFORE_FALLBACK_OFF=1` disable). Practical CLI parity matches greedy/default-off token IDs on high-accept and rejection prompts; the partial-reject smoke improves `The quick brown fox` from `25.99 -> 25.09 ms/tok` by avoiding one draft resync, while high-accept is neutral/noisy.
  - [x] Falsifier/narrowing: raising neural speculative `QWEN35_SPEC_PLAIN_FALLBACK_GAMMA` from default `2` to `8` is not a robust default win. It helps some `bootstrap_gamma=16` cases by skipping draft backups and falling back sooner after rejection (`The capital of France is` around `15.37 ms/tok`; `def fibonacci(n):` around `21.38 ms/tok` instead of `24.5` with fallback `2`), but no-bootstrap high-accept was neutral/noisy and one adjacent run regressed (`17.67` default-8 vs `17.50` old-2). Keep default `2`; use `fallback_gamma>=8` only with explicit bootstrap/high-accept tuning.
  - [x] Falsifier: disabling auto n-gram after 16 consecutive no-candidate plain steps preserves exact output but is not a useful speed policy. It leaves high-repeat behavior intact, but no-repeat `def fibonacci(n):` stayed slightly slower (`20.29 ms/tok` with miss-limit vs `20.22 ms/tok` without) and the policy can suppress late n-gram wins, so no source change was retained.
  - [x] Add the harness-compatible `QWEN35_SPEC_PLAIN_FALLBACK_OFF=1` escape hatch to `qwen35_generate` without changing defaults. The CLI now reports whether target-only fallback is enabled, and the skip-draft-before-fallback optimization is gated by the same fallback policy so fallback-off A/B remains semantically clean.
  - [x] Make the practical CLI n-gram rollback backup state lazy: `qwen35_generate` now allocates the backup only when a candidate chunk actually appears. This is exact and avoids a large unused state allocation on no-candidate auto/ngram runs; parity still matches greedy for high-repeat, partial-repeat, and no-repeat 32-token smokes, though wall timing is neutral/noisy.
  - [x] Add default-preserving CLI trace suppression (`QWEN35_TRACE_STEPS_OFF=1` or `QWEN35_QUIET=1`) for benchmark/generation runs. Summaries and final output stay visible, but per-token/per-cycle logs are suppressed; greedy, auto, and neural speculative 32-token parity smokes still match baseline token IDs on high-repeat, partial-repeat, and no-repeat prompts.
  - [x] Port the harness gamma=1 accepted-token fast path into `qwen35_generate` (`QWEN35_SPEC_SINGLE_FAST_OFF=1` disables). The practical CLI now bypasses rollback backup and chunk verification when `draft_next == target_next` at `gamma=1`, preserving exact token IDs; default target-only fallback means the path is mostly an A/B/control win for fallback-off speculative runs rather than a default speed lever.
  - [x] Align the neural speculative acceptance harness with practical CLI state setup: `bin/qwen35_speculative_accept.cr` now prepares target/draft/backup Metal state buffers by default and reports `prepare_state=...`; `QWEN35_PREPARE_STATE_OFF=1` preserves the old lazy-allocation path for A/B. High-accept 16-token smoke stays exact and reduces backup/copy timing noise (`target_backup ~2.2 ms`, `draft_backup ~0.4 ms` prepared vs `~5.3/~1.8 ms` lazy in the adjacent run).
  - [x] Fresh speculative attribution: high-accept neural speculation with bootstrap32/full-row guard reaches `11.49 ms/tok` for 64 generated tokens (`87.04 tok/s`, `100%` accepted), but time is now draft-dominated (`draft=451.3 ms`, `target_verify=274.6 ms`). Partial-reject `The quick brown fox` correctly falls back after one cycle and is parity/slightly slower than plain target (`22.33` vs `22.11 ms/tok`), so the next exact neural-spec win needs a faster/smaller draft or fewer draft steps, not just verifier micro-tuning.
  - [x] Falsifier: reducing Q8_0 top1 rows-per-threadgroup from `12` to `8` looked faster in draft sync profile (`~7.31-7.44 ms/tok` vs `~7.65-7.74 ms/tok` on repeated 64-token draft runs), but failed exact speculative parity on `The capital of France is` with bootstrap32/full-row guard. The source was reverted to `12`; Q8 top1 tile-shape changes require end-to-end speculative parity, not just draft-only speed.
  - [x] Falsifier: fusing Q8_0 dual FFN gate/up GEMV with SwiGLU activation removed the `ffn_act` trace rows but regressed draft wall. Focused specs passed with `QWEN35_Q8_FFN_SWIGLU_FUSED=1`, yet paired 64-token draft profiles favored the existing separate activation path (`~7.73-7.81 ms/tok` off vs `~7.75-8.18 ms/tok` fused). The temporary kernel/route was removed; like the earlier Q4 pair+SwiGLU branch, putting `exp` into the projection kernel is not a speed path.
  - [x] Add practical CLI neural verifier selection (`QWEN35_SPEC_VERIFY=chunk-inplace|hybrid|serial`). Default remains `chunk-inplace`; opt-in `hybrid` verifies the first speculative cycle serially and then returns to chunking, improving first-cycle partial-reject smokes (`Once upon a time` `22.84/23.06 -> 22.48/22.11 ms/tok`, `The quick brown fox` `22.86/22.85 -> 22.12/22.22`) while regressing high-accept prompts (`The capital of France is` `16.33/16.40 -> 16.94/16.72`).
  - [x] Falsifier: a Q8_0 mixed dual GEMV for unequal recurrent draft projections (`qkv 1024x6144` + `gate 1024x2048`) reduced `rec.proj` encode time but regressed 0.8B draft wall (`~7.39-7.47 ms/tok` separate vs `~7.62-7.70` mixed), so the temporary kernel/route was removed. Dispatch fusion alone is not enough for this Q8 projection shape.
  - [x] Add exact recurrent prefill shared-H16 projection input: Q5 qkv and Q4 gate GEMMs now share one F32→F16 conversion (`QWEN35_REC_PROJ_SHARED_H16_OFF=1` disables). Focused Qwen/Metal specs pass (`23 examples, 0 failures`); paired A/B is neutral at pp64 (`150.45 ms` default vs `150.55 ms` off, `4/8` wins) and small-positive at pp256/pp1024 (`483.28 vs 484.48 ms`, `4/6`; `1916.54 vs 1917.83 ms`, `3/3`) while reducing profiled conversion traffic by one recurrent `4096*b` conversion per recurrent layer.
  - [x] Re-check llama.cpp Q4_K prefill GEMM source against the current local Q4_H16 route. llama.cpp still uses the same 64x32/NK32/4-simdgroup simdgroup-matrix `kernel_mul_mm_q4_K_f32` family; the local route already keeps the same tile shape and adds prepared F16 input reuse. Prepared-state pp64 A/B confirms the H16 route is still material (`145.99 ms` default vs `148.07 ms` with `QWEN35_Q4K_H16_GEMM_OFF=1`, `6/6` wins), so there is no obvious llama.cpp Q4 tile trick left to copy.
  - [x] Add opt-in intra-group prefill phase profiling (`QWEN35_PREFILL_PHASE_PROFILE=1`) for `full+rec` grouped command buffers. It intentionally inserts profiling command-buffer boundaries after the full-attention layer and after each recurrent layer, so it is attribution-only, not a production route. pp64 prepared profile shows each `full+rec` group is almost exactly four similar phases: full layer `~4.39-4.46 ms`, each recurrent layer `~4.50-4.61 ms`; default env-off profile remains unchanged around `142.96 ms` p50. This refutes a hidden single bad phase inside the grouped wave.
  - [x] Falsifier: exact compact DeltaNet summaries do not currently beat the production rowwise chunk even as a final-state lower bound. `qwen35_deltanet_compact_vs_rowwise_micro` measured compact vs rowwise p50 at block8 `0.3375` vs `0.2665 ms`, block16 `0.4396` vs `0.2831 ms`, and block32 `0.3536` vs `0.2636 ms`; row-threadgroup apply was also slower/neutral. Row-basis summary composition itself is exact and tileable, but the block-scan model says rank growth/composition cost only becomes attractive for long prompts with a much better integrated compact scan. Do not integrate compact summaries into first-run prefill yet.
  - [x] Falsifier: Q4_K per-subblock 16-entry dequant lookup tables are exact but slower. A temporary `dequantize_q4_K_fn` LUT variant passed focused Qwen/Metal specs (`23 examples, 0 failures`), but hot-shape attribution for Q4 `4096x12288 b64` worsened from `1.600 ms` after reverting to the current arithmetic path to `3.748 ms` with the LUT; the smaller Q4 `4096x4096 b64` shape also worsened (`0.661 ms` current vs `0.753 ms` LUT). Do not add nibble lookup/precompute inside the current Q4 GEMM kernel; register/local pressure dominates the saved multiply.
  - [x] Falsifier: changing Q4_K GEMM dequant arithmetic from float to half is neutral and weakens numeric margin. Keep the current float expression with one final half rounding.
  - [x] Falsifier: removing inner `simdgroup_barrier(mem_none)` calls from Q5/Q6 f32-output prefill GEMM kernels is not a win. Keep the barriers, matching the earlier Q4 barrier falsifier.

Keep the current fail-cheap behavior: after low-confidence early rejects, spending draft cycles to improve acceptance can be slower than letting target steps dominate.
  - [x] Add `bin/qwen35_speculative_sweep.cr`, a measurement-only multi-prompt policy sweep for the speculative acceptance harness. It runs a compiled `qwen35_speculative_accept` runner across named policies (`default`, `guard`, `bootstrap32`, `bootstrap32_guard`, `hybrid`, `ngram`, `ngram_guard`), parses `ms/tok`, acceptance, gamma stats, and plain-target baseline, then prints per-prompt rows plus policy averages. The sweep now interleaves policies inside each prompt/repetition block to reduce host-drift bias; an interleaved smoke with `--tokens 8 --reps 2 --policies default,bootstrap32,ngram` ranked default and n-gram near parity (`1.274x`/`1.267x`) while bootstrap32 lagged (`1.213x`), demonstrating why policy changes need multi-prompt wall-time sweeps instead of one-prompt acceptance claims.
  - [x] Add `--dump-cycles JSONL` to the neural/ngram speculative harness. `bin/qwen35_speculative_accept.cr` now writes per-cycle JSONL with target/draft model ids, prompt hash, position, policy/kind, verifier mode, gamma, proposed/accepted counts, reject index, n-gram match features, timing breakdowns, candidate hash, and post-run `expected_gain_ms` versus plain target ms/token. Raw token ids are omitted by default and require explicit `--dump-cycle-token-ids` / `QWEN35_SPEC_DUMP_TOKEN_IDS=1`. Verified with high-accept neural, token-id opt-in, and forced n-gram-cycle smokes.

A 2-prompt/2-policy smoke produced 4/4 JSONL files, including both neural and n-gram cycle records.

A smoke summarized `neural/neural` and `ngram/ngram` rows separately, giving immediate signal for router feature work without a separate notebook.
  - [x] Add prompt labels and a privacy-preserving manifest to cycle-dump sweeps. `--prompt` / `--only-prompts` entries can now use `NAME::TEXT`; dump filenames include the safe prompt label, and `prompt_manifest.jsonl` records `prompt_index`, `prompt_name`, prompt hash, and byte length. Raw prompt text remains opt-in via `--dump-prompt-texts`; default sweep output now prints labels instead of raw prompt previews.
  - [x] Add named warm-aware gamma policies to the sweep: `router16` (`gamma4`, cap `max_gamma=16`, jump to `16` after one full accept), `fixed16`, and n-gram variants. These are measurement aliases over existing exact verifier controls, not new decode semantics. Fake-runner and real-runner smokes verified policy selection, labeled dump filenames, and cycle summaries.

    - [x] Warm-aware gamma gate: fixed `gamma16/tokens8` with 2B draft wins high-accept prompts but loses the 4-prompt mix (`default 0.849x`, `ngram 0.909x`) because `France` and partial `quick_fox` rejects dominate. Router policy must avoid low-accept chunks and reserve large gamma for longer/high-confidence spans.

This is still an associative affine transform over state, but naive dense block summaries would be too expensive; the viable research path is compact low-rank/WY-style block summaries plus per-block serial replay, not direct `conv1d/cumsum`.

## Deferred research backlog — efficient attention / long context

- [ ] **R.1** FlashAttention-style exact full-attention prefill kernel for Qwen35 full-attn layers; first add intra-group phase attribution and only implement if `full.attn` is material at pp2048+.
  - [x] Falsifier/current priority: pp2048 prefill attribution shows full-attention is not the wall yet (`attn wait 26.35 ms` inside `~10.17 s` profiled wall). The material wall is recurrent grouped prefill / DeltaNet serial work (`dn wait ~9991.69 ms`) plus large conversion traffic (`10.85 GiB`, 73% logical traffic), so FlashAttention is deferred until recurrent scan and conversion pressure are addressed.
- [ ] **R.2** DeepSeek Sparse Attention / NSA-style indexer experiment; requires calibration + sparse adaptation, not an exact drop-in for existing Qwen weights.
- [ ] **R.3** Linear-attention replacement track; treat as new architecture or distillation/continued-training project, not an inference-only optimization.
- [ ] **R.4** Training-free long-context inference hacks under eval gates: attention sinks / sliding window, SnapKV/H2O-like KV retention, and KV quantization. Keep all default-off until LongBench/RULER/top-logit drift evidence exists.
- [ ] **R.5** Exact DeltaNet associative block-scan prototype for long prefill.
  - [x] First prove equivalence on tiny CPU shapes (`s=8`) against the serial `delta_net_step!` recurrence: `spec/qwen35_deltanet_affine_scan_spec.cr` verifies both composed affine final state and block-prefix replay intermediate outputs (`2 examples, 0 failures`).
  - [x] Estimate work for `s=128` and prompt lengths `64/256/1024/2048` with a `1.25x` continuation gate: `bin/qwen35_deltanet_scan_model.cr` shows naive dense summaries fail, while compact summaries can pass pp256 if one summary composition stays below about `38-47` serial-token-equivalent DeltaNet steps for block sizes `8/16/32`.
  - [x] Add a dense CPU block-scan baseline (`bin/qwen35_deltanet_dense_scan_cpu.cr`) for exactness/overhead checks before Metal work. It reproduces serial final state on `s=16,tokens=64,block=16` and `s=32,tokens=128,block=32` with max state deltas below `1e-16`, but sequential CPU dense summary+replay is `~12-19x` slower than serial, confirming dense summaries are only plausible as a parallel long-prefill GPU baseline, not a short-prompt CPU-style algorithm.
  - [x] Extract reusable block-scan algebra into `ML::GGUF::Qwen35DeltaNetBlockScan`, so the proof spec and dense baseline share the same serial, affine, composition, replay, and compact-additive-summary formulas before any Metal prototype.
  - [x] Prototype compact block summaries using dense `A` plus low-rank `B = Σ left_i ⊗ right_i`, including compact apply and compact summary composition without materializing dense `B`. Focused specs pass (`8 examples, 0 failures`); sequential CPU compact is still slower than serial but faster than dense-summary CPU on checked shapes, and the storage model becomes favorable for Qwen shape `s=128` with block sizes below `64`.
  - [x] Prototype compact/WY-style transition summaries for `A`: `A = gamma * (I + U V^T)` now has exact build, apply, and composition checks. Focused specs pass (`11 examples, 0 failures`); the `--fully` CPU baseline avoids dense `A/B` materialization and is exact, but still sequentially slower than serial. This keeps the Metal path alive while confirming CPU-style execution is not the win.
  - [x] Build default-off Metal microbench for fully compact summary apply at synthetic Qwen shape (`s=128`, block `8/16/32/64`). The two-kernel `coeffs + apply` probe is exact within f32 drift (`max_delta < 3e-8`) and measures p50 `~0.257/0.269/0.293/0.322 ms` for block `8/16/32/64`; this excludes summary construction and prefill integration, so it is a green apply-kernel gate, not a full scan speedup claim.
  - [x] Add Metal compact-`A` construction microbench (`bin/qwen35_deltanet_compact_build_metal_micro.cr`). It is exact within f32 drift but only becomes faster than CPU construction at larger blocks: p50 `~0.223/0.281/0.442/1.108 ms` for block `8/16/32/64`, with CPU-build speedup `0.07x/0.24x/2.13x/1.24x`. This says block `32` is the first plausible Metal construction point; block `8/16` need fusion with apply/replay or they lose to dispatch/setup overhead.
  - [x] Add Metal compact-`B` right-factor construction microbench (`bin/qwen35_deltanet_compact_b_metal_micro.cr`). The direct per-rank vector kernel is exact within f32 drift (`max_right_delta < 5e-8`) and measures p50 `~1.206/1.086/2.034/2.835 ms` for block `8/16/32/64`; this makes compact-`B` construction the dominant current overhead and points to block `16` as the best early candidate before replay/scan costs.
  - [x] Falsifier: current compact block-scan lower-bound loses badly to `delta_net_chunk_128_rowwise` before replay/integration. The synthetic `build(A+B)+apply(final state)` path is exact within f32 drift, but at `s=128` block `8/16/32` p50 is `~1.300/2.324/3.566 ms` versus rowwise `~0.270/0.287/0.318 ms`. Since rowwise also produces per-token outputs and compact currently does not, do not integrate this compact-summary design into prefill without a new fused construction/replay algorithm.
  - [x] Apply the LTP/WBA frame to compact-`B` construction: a threadgroup-wide right-factor kernel parallelizes the `s=128` vector recurrence and remains exact within f32 drift (`max_right_tg_delta ~3.2e-8`). At block `16`, the microbench improved scalar compact-`B` p50 from `~2.18 ms` to `~0.30 ms` in a formatted rerun (`~7.2x`), with earlier short sweeps showing `~4.8-11.3x` over blocks `8/16/32`. This validates compact-`B` as an LTP/WBA target.
  - [x] Falsifier/update: replacing the separate compact `coeffs + apply` stages with a row-threadgroup apply kernel did not improve the full lower-bound; block `16` stayed around `~0.42-0.52 ms` and still failed to robustly beat rowwise. Keep the row-threadgroup apply route opt-in for research only; the next useful branch is multi-block parallel summary build + prefix/replay, not more single-block apply fusion.
  - [x] Add a multi-block summary-build gate to `bin/qwen35_deltanet_compact_b_metal_micro.cr`. At block `16`, compact `A+B` summary construction for all blocks in parallel is exact within f32 drift and becomes cheaper than rowwise for long prompts: blocks `16/64` measured summary-build p50 `~0.338/0.235 ms` vs rowwise chunk `~0.496/0.790 ms`. Blocks `1/4` still lose or remain marginal, so this is long-prefill-only evidence.

Uncapped compact prefix composition is a dead end for long prompts (`pp1024` speedup `~0.28x`, `pp2048` `~0.14x`) because rank grows `16 -> ... -> tokens`. An optimistic exact rank cap at `s=128` makes `pp1024/pp2048` plausible (`~1.71x/~2.66x`) but still does not pass `1.25x` at `pp256`; the next prototype must include exact rank/basis compression or avoid rank-growing summary composition entirely.

This keeps adjoint replay alive, but term construction is still excluded.

Keep adjoint replay as an algebraic tool, not the next production prefill path.

On a varied 80-token prompt, per-layer rank-64 PCA does not make residuals small (`p50` residual roughly `0.49/0.34/0.42` for layers `0/8/16`), but full-model drift is surprisingly tolerant: approximating one layer at a time gives `97.5-100%` teacher-forced top1 and `100%` 12-token greedy top1; approximating layers `0,8,16` together gives `97.5%` teacher-forced top1 and `93.75%` 16-token greedy top1. Approximating sparse recurrent layers `0,8,16` gives `98.44%` teacher-forced top1 and `100%` 12-token greedy top1 for both rank-32/64; approximating early recurrent layers `0,1,2,4,5,6` gives `100%` teacher-forced and `100%` 12-token greedy top1, with rank-64 reducing KL/max-delta (`mean_kl 0.0036` vs `0.0121`). If `K≈Bc`, the DeltaNet state can be stored as `M B^T` with shape `128 x rank` and outputs use `M*(B^T q)`, giving a plausible approximate speed path independent of exact associative scan.
  - [x] Prove the projected-K low-rank DeltaNet state formula on CPU: for fixed per-head basis `B` and `K_proj = Bc`, the full projected recurrence is exactly represented by `S = M B^T`, update `M = gM + beta*(V - gM c)c^T`, and output `Y = M*(B^T Q)`. The probe verifies low-rank `M` against full projected `128x128` recurrence with proof drift around `1e-8` for outputs and `<=1.15e-5` for reconstructed state. Residual-gated fallback at threshold `0.8` restores adversary `top1_match=100%` with mean cosine `0.99972` for `0,2` at `29.17%` approximate steps and `0.99881` for `0,2,4` at `36.11%` approximate steps. Leaf rescue alone is insufficient; the next tree experiment must avoid paying for the wrong tail.

Under the conservative cost model, `varied` stays near fixed `gamma=16` (`1.169x` vs `1.174x`), while the math falsifier recovers the fixed `gamma=4/8` economics (`1.092x` vs `0.890x` for fixed `gamma=16`) by reducing proposed/verifier tokens from `41` to `33`. With the same conservative unit costs, progressive `4,4,8` estimates jump from `~1.17x/1.09x` (sum model) to `1.53x` on `varied` and `1.41x` on `math_paris`.
