Adding `after_full_accepts=2` did not rescue `gen32` (`-24.4%` mean, min `-42.76%`, accept `85.53%`). Decision: do not super-fuse this route. Previous-chunk margin/streak are insufficient; the next router must include richer pre-submit features or a cheap agreement/probe signal. Logs: `/tmp/qwen36_updown_router_abba_m20_gen32_suite4_release_20260503.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_updown_router_m20_gen64_code_reason_release_20260503.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_updown_router_wide_gen64_json_m20_release_20260503.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_updown_router_m20_after2_gen32_suite4_release_20260503.log`.
  - [x] Test same-boundary lowrank-vs-pca-updown agreement as the next router primitive. Implemented `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-updown-agreement-gate` and `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-updown-agreement-steps=N`, using forked probe states so diagnostics do not contaminate final draft state. Result: one-step agreement is too weak (9B: `3/3` agreement passes but pca route still rejects inside a `gamma=2` block); two-step prefix agreement restores short-smoke acceptance but is far too expensive (9B `377.2ms` vs lowrank `171.123ms`, 27B `1060.432ms` vs lowrank `480.386ms`). Decision: keep agreement probes as diagnostics/router-feature mining only, not a production route or super-fusion target. Log: `/tmp/qwen36_updown_agree_steps2_gen4_20260503.log`.
  - [x] Mine cheaper pca-updown router features before trying more online controllers. Added agreement-margin sweeps (`--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-updown-agreement-margin-thresholds=LIST`) and held-out FFN up/down reconstruction features (`--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-ffn-updown-route-features`). Margin-only is not a reliable standalone selector: a 9B short smoke had the failing pca block with higher lowrank margin than the passing block (`fail_avg=0.2836` vs `pass_avg=0.1773`), while an easier 9B `gen8` row passed all four checks with high margin; 27B short gate had no failures but thresholds `>=0.5` selected nothing. FFN pca-updown residual features are useful as a cheap warning signal: held-out reconstruction is very rough on both 9B and 27B (`rel_rmse_mean ~=0.96..1.00`, low cosine), even when the downstream logit drift is still top1-clean. Decision: use these as offline/router-training diagnostics, not online gates. Verification: `/tmp/qwen35_agree_margin_probe` build + `8 examples, 0 failures`; `/tmp/qwen35_ffn_updown_features_probe` build + `8 examples, 0 failures`; logs `/tmp/qwen35_updown_agree_margin_steps2_smoke_20260503.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_updown_agree_margin_steps2_gen8_20260503.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_updown_agree_margin_steps2_gen4_20260503.log`.
  - [x] Test the first feature-derived pca-updown router hypothesis across prompt classes. 9B wide late band (`24,25,26,28,29,30`, rank32, external FFN adapter, `schedule=4,4,8`) showed a real short-horizon split at `gen16`: `code/json` had lower K residual + FFN rel-RMSE (`0.236/0.772`, `0.242/0.784`) and pca-updown stayed clean/slightly faster (`+1.42%`, `+1.97%`), while `main/reasoning` had higher features (`0.281/0.838`, `0.307/0.887`) and pca-updown rejected heavily (`52%/56%` accept). The same features fail as a simple static router at `gen32`: all pca-updown rows regressed, including `code/json` (`76.92%/72.5%` accept, `-32.81%/-26.06%` wall). A focused 27B narrow-band (`24,25,26`) `gen16` run also refuted absolute thresholds as portable: features still separated prompt classes, but pca-updown regressed aggregate (`baseline_delta_mean=-29.97%`, min `-52.33%`) with only JSON slightly positive (`+2.72%`) because baseline already had one reject. Decision: a viable router needs horizon/position/model/band features and verifier-outcome training; do not promote hand-written residual/FFN thresholds. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_router_features_suite_gen16_20260503093932.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_router_features_suite_gen32_20260503094302.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_router_features_narrow_gen16_20260503094658.log`.
  - [x] Test pca-updown burst caps and layer-mask dependence. Added `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-updown-max-chunks=N` to cap how many draft chunks may use pca-updown before falling back to lowrank. This strongly supports the accumulated-drift hypothesis on 9B wide late band at `gen32`: always-on pca-updown previously regressed badly, while `max_chunks=1` preserved parity/acceptance and became slightly positive (`baseline_delta_mean=+1.23%`, min `-0.08%`), `max_chunks=2` improved similarly (`+1.62%`, min `-0.25%`), and `max_chunks=4` crossed the cliff again (`-47.29%`, min `-91.34%`, accept mean `83.73%`). Layer masks are prompt-dependent: with `max_chunks=2`, `24,25,26` was safe/neutral (`accept_mean=97.14%`, min `-1.85%`), while `28,29,30` caused a main-prompt reject and aggregate regression (`accept_mean=96.36%`, min `-24.35%`). Focused 27B narrow-band `gen16` did not inherit the 9B burst win: `max_chunks=1/2` still regressed aggregate (`-23.34%/-23.35%`) because main/code reject on the first pca chunk, while JSON/reasoning were near neutral/slightly positive. Decision: pca-updown is a short-burst candidate on 9B, but 27B needs pre-submit prompt/layer gating before the first pca chunk; next router should choose both `max_chunks` and `layer_mask` by expected replay risk. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_max_pca_chunks1_wide_gen32_20260503121914.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_max_pca_chunks2_wide_gen32_20260503122158.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_max_pca_chunks4_wide_gen32_20260503122453.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_layer_mask_24_25_26_max2_gen32_20260503122808.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_layer_mask_28_29_30_max2_gen32_20260503123051.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_max_pca_chunks1_narrow_gen16_20260503123348.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_max_pca_chunks2_narrow_gen16_20260503123729.log`.
  - [x] Test pca-updown exact refresh/no-carry after accepted chunks. Added `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-updown-refresh-on-accept`, which skips pre-submitting the next block after a fully accepted pca-updown chunk and reseeds from exact verifier state; paired serial mirrors the same policy. Build/spec/release gates passed. The hypothesis is refuted as a speed route: 9B wide late band `gen32/max_chunks=4` is less catastrophic but still deeply negative (`-31.96%` vs prior `-47.29%`, accept still `83.73%`); 9B `max_chunks=2` loses the previous small win (`+1.62%` no-refresh -> `-4.86%` refresh) because exact reseed breaks overlap; 27B narrow `max_chunks=1` improves the damage (`-23.34%` -> `-9.35%`) but still regresses and code still rejects. Decision: pca state carry is not the root blocker; first-chunk risk and overlap loss dominate. Keep refresh default-off as a diagnostic/refutation, and route pca-updown with cheap pre-submit risk prediction instead of exact refresh. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_refresh_accept_wide_gen32_max4_20260503151956.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_refresh_accept_wide_gen32_max2_20260503152307.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_refresh_accept_narrow_gen16_max1_20260503152649.log`.
  - [x] Test alternate 27B pca-updown layer masks after the refresh refutation. Focused Qwen3.6-27B `gen16/max_chunks=1` gates show the first-chunk risk is not solved by moving the pca band. `28,29,30` avoids the main-prompt reject but still regresses aggregate (`baseline_delta_mean=-9.21%`, min `-36.48%`) because code rejects; wide `24,25,26,28,29,30` improves JSON/reasoning (`+3.02%/+12.63%`) but regresses main/code heavily (`-52.30%/-40.14%`) and aggregate (`-19.2%`). Decision: layer-mask choice is prompt-dependent and can create positive rows, but no static 27B pca mask is ready. Next pca route needs a prompt/layer risk router; otherwise prioritize MTP exact-resync / branch-state verifier work. Logs: `/tmp/qwen36_layer_mask_28_29_30_max1_gen16_20260503153452.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_layer_mask_wide_max1_gen16_20260503153902.log`.
  - [ ] Design the next pca-updown router only if it can predict the current pca proposal risk without running both candidate bodies online: candidates are prompt/position class features, route residuals, cheap distilled agreement predictor, pca proposal margin/offramp before pre-submitting the next block, and/or per-prompt learned thresholds from route-feature rows. Promotion gate remains release ABBA/repeats with positive min-delta across main/code/json/reasoning at `gen32/64`; otherwise prioritize MTP exact-resync or branch-state verifier work over pca-updown super-fusion.
  - [ ] Build the first fused/resident Metal MTP path: keep `fc/v/qgate/o/gate/up/down` intermediates GPU-resident, fuse RMSNorm/SwiGLU/gating where practical, shared target embedding/output, and topK output. Baseline target is correctness + acceptance first, then compare wall speed against current same-weight self-draft and llama.cpp speculative MTP if available. Note: existing one-token body fusion only helps stateless/no-cache MTP and is not enough for the wall loop because cached multi-token MTP needs K/V updates.
  - [ ] Build a fused/resident target verifier prototype: preserve exact verification, but avoid per-group CPU materialization in `prefill_tokens_hidden_top1s`; candidate designs are a chunk-major command buffer over full+recurrent layer groups, or branch-state snapshots at candidate boundaries to remove reject replay. Gate: parity against greedy plus 3-prompt/ABBA wall comparison before promotion.
  - [x] Test the cheap exact early-stop verifier falsifier before the larger fused verifier rewrite. Added `--mtp-spec-wall-serial-early-verify`, which verifies each MTP wall stage token-by-token and stops at the first mismatch, preserving parity while measuring the lower bound of avoiding wrong-tail verifier/replay work. The 27B natural `france/code/reason`, `gen16/gamma8/stage3/stage_once/lazy` gate refuted this as a speed route: default chunk verifier was `wall_ms=3931.311`, `plain_speedup=0.729`, `verifier_ms=2695.444`, `replay_ms=531.336`, while serial-early removed replay and cut verifier tokens (`60 -> 48`) but regressed to `wall_ms=6385.100`, `plain_speedup=0.459`, `verifier_ms=5603.053`. Decision: keep the flag default-off as a control-flow refutation. The next real implementation must preserve chunk fusion while exposing correction-boundary state, i.e. chunk-major resident verifier or branch-state snapshots, not serial exact body replay.
  - [x] Add the branch-state snapshot cost probe before writing snapshot kernels. `--mtp-spec-wall-snapshot-cost-probe` simulates one recurrent-state blit per verified row after wall timing and reports `snapshot_sim_ms` separately, so the normal wall number is not polluted. On Qwen3.6-27B, one correction-boundary recurrent snapshot is about `149.75 MiB` (`48` recurrent layers; full-attn KV rows are only `0.125 MiB/token` and can be ignored by position). The natural `france/code/reason`, `gen16/gamma8/stage3/stage_once/lazy` gate measured `60` simulated recurrent snapshots at `135.742ms` versus `590.092ms` replay. Decision: branch-state snapshots are memory-heavy but viable enough to prototype; expected replay saving is around `~450ms` on this gate, so snapshots alone will not beat plain decode, but they are a necessary component when combined with better routing/verifier fusion.
  - [x] Add snapshot ceiling accounting to the MTP wall summaries. When `--mtp-spec-wall-snapshot-cost-probe` is enabled, rows now report `snapshot_modeled_wall_ms = wall_ms - replay_ms + snapshot_sim_ms` and `snapshot_modeled_speedup`. This is a lower-bound/counterfactual, not a real speed claim, but it prevents us from building snapshot kernels for controller policies that still cannot win after replay is removed. Fresh sequential checks show the model is useful but noisy: `offramp=1` stayed below plain on the 3-prompt gate (`snapshot_modeled_speedup=0.781`), while `offramp=2` initially printed `1.144` only in a run where `plain_exact_ms` was anomalously high. Repeated `offramp=2` B-cases refuted that promotion (`snapshot_modeled_speedup=0.699/0.783`, parity true). Decision: no current scalar `offramp` controller is a good snapshot-kernel target; next target should be a better high-accept router or a fused verifier execution-model change.
  - [x] Falsify naive chunk-major MTP verification before a wide Metal rewrite. Current prefill verifier already fuses one full-attention layer plus the following recurrent run into one command buffer per layer group; the remaining cost is mostly repeated FFN/projection weight traffic on small verifier chunks. A full `stage=0` gamma chunk is not the answer: it increases wrong-tail proposal/verification (`draft_tokens=120`, `verifier_tokens=131`) and only reaches `snapshot_modeled_speedup=0.442` on the natural 27B gate. Guard-stage sweep with snapshot counterfactual keeps `stage=3 + stage_once + lazy` as best scalar policy (`snapshot_modeled_speedup=0.770`), while `stage=2/4/5` are worse (`0.566/0.672/0.616`). Decision: do not start a broad fused/chunk-major Metal rewrite for the current low-accept MTP controller. The fused/chunk-major target needs either a high-accept pre-submit router/candidate source or a verifier that can batch only likely-correct spans.
  - [x] Add GPU-backed no-norm MTP hidden top2 projection as a fused/chunk-major prerequisite. `Qwen35Metal.project_top2_no_norm` reuses the existing Q6/Q8 top2 tile kernels and `qwen35_top2_reduce_tiles`, and `Qwen35MTP.hidden_top2` now avoids the CPU/full-logits fallback for `--mtp-chain-topk 2` unless `QWEN35_MTP_TOP2_METAL_OFF=1`. Correctness is covered by a new no-norm top2 spec against full logits plus the existing decode top2 spec (`9 examples, 0 failures`). A 27B teacher chain smoke preserved the same candidate ids/rank histogram with and without the Metal top2 path (`top1_hits=11/16`, `topk_hits=14/16`, `rank_hist=miss:2,1:11,2:3`). Timing is not a standalone speed win because the MTP body still dominates (`mtp_avg_ms ~=10ms`); the value is removing an oracle/CPU full-logits seam so future MTP K2/router/tree verifier experiments can stay GPU-resident.   - [x] Add and refute an exact staged verifier bonus probe (`--mtp-spec-wall-stage-bonus`). The idea was to preserve chunk verifier fusion while emitting one exact verifier bonus token after each accepted MTP stage, then restart from that exact boundary instead of continuing a drift-prone MTP tail. It preserved parity, but the 27B natural `gen16/gamma8/stage3/stage_once/lazy` gate regressed badly: baseline `plain_speedup=0.692`, `snapshot_modeled_speedup=0.804`, `passes=17`, `verifier_tokens=60`, `replay_tokens=9`; stage-bonus fell to `plain_speedup=0.503`, `snapshot_modeled_speedup=0.666`, `passes=20`, `verifier_tokens=76`, `replay_tokens=28`. Verdict: verifier bonus on every stage is the wrong chunk-major shape; it increases extra verified rows and replay after early mismatches. Keep the flag default-off as a falsifier, not a tuning target. Logs: `/tmp/qwen36_mtp_stage_bonus_baseline_20260504_201125.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_mtp_stage_bonus_on_20260504_201215.log`.

It requests MTP K2 during wall drafting, leaves exact output semantics unchanged, and counts reject corrections where the exact target token equals the MTP second candidate plus the associated wrong-tail/replay tax. On 27B natural `gen16`, top2 covered `5/14` reject corrections and `328.646ms` of `605.311ms` replay; on `gen32`, it covered `13/31` and `1140.957ms` of `2813.53ms` replay. This is a real signal, but not enough alone: even deleting all top2-associated replay from the `gen32` wall would still leave the route far below plain decode (`plain_speedup=0.395` before, about `0.427` under that narrow counterfactual; snapshot-modeled is only `0.477`). Verdict: do not build a top2-only branch-state kernel as the next standalone feature. Use top2 as a component of a broader high-accept router / branch-state verifier where accepted chunks stay chunk-major and rejects avoid replay. It auto-enables top2 accounting, and after a reject whose exact correction is not the MTP top2 candidate, it stops paying MTP/verifier/replay for the suffix and finishes with exact greedy target decode from the current boundary. Same-binary 27B natural `gen32/gamma8/stage3/stage_once/lazy` A/B preserved parity and moved the suite from `plain_speedup=0.592` / `wall_ms=9968.344` without the off-ramp to `plain_speedup=0.900` / `wall_ms=6583.273` with the off-ramp. This is still below plain exact, so it is not a default speed path; the useful finding is that top2-miss is a strong router/fail-closed signal that avoids repeated bad MTP cycles. Combining with `reject_offramp=1` is not a robust new route: it measured `0.892x` in one run and `0.986x` in a top2-miss+off1 run where same-run plain exact was much slower, so this is timing-noise/near-plain territory rather than a clean win. Verdict: fail-closed routing is useful, but beating plain requires eliminating the initial MTP/verifier overhead or routing only high-accept spans before submit. The first-step signal looked plausible (`reason` exact token was MTP top2 at margin `0.216`), but route-level A/B refutes simple thresholds: `F=0.5` regressed gen32 top2-miss from `plain_speedup=0.913` to `0.784`, and `F=0.25` still regressed the code row (`0.857`) while the suite's apparent `1.000x` depended on anomalously slow plain exact timing. - **Latest Metal exact-refresh bridge:** static layer-mask variants on the schema falsifier did not remove the GPU reject, confirming that layer selection alone is not the missing bridge (`0,2,4`, pairs, and singles all kept the miss). Added default-off `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-exact-refresh=N` plus `lowrank_reconstruct_state`: a draft wave can reconstruct low-rank DN state into full SSM state, run exact recurrent DN for selected low-rank layers, then project back to low-rank without CPU readback. A first attempt was still negative until the probe tracked whether full state was already current; otherwise exact state was overwritten by low-rank reconstruction. With freshness tracking, schema `exact_refresh=1` reached `100%` accept/parity (`overlap_ms=635.105`, `plain_speedup=0.592`) versus the prior reject (`88.24%`, `overlap_ms≈845-869`). Interval sweep showed only refresh-every-step fixed this schema miss (`N=2/3/4/8` still rejected), so this is an upper-bound/state-handoff proof, not a speed default. A 3-prompt gate kept parity: code/reason were already clean and refresh mostly changed wall noise, while schema improved `88.24%/1246.644ms -> 100%/795.319ms`. Next step: make exact refresh selective with a GPU-resident or preplanned `update=beta*residual` risk score; do not promote interval-1 exact refresh as a production route.
- **Latest exact-refresh selectivity gate:** added default-off manual selectors `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-exact-refresh-offsets=LIST` and `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-exact-refresh-prefix=N`. On the schema falsifier, no single offset `0..15` fixed the miss; prefix `0..7` and `0..9` still rejected, while prefix `0..10` / `prefix=11` reached `100%` accept/parity. Suffix `4..15` still rejected. Interpretation: the miss is not a one-token rescue; the draft needs early recurrent state kept exact/fresh through about the first 11 generated waves, then later low-rank can continue for this prompt. This is still not a speed default, but it narrows the next router hypothesis from "refresh rare point" to "warm exact-refresh prefix or state-freshness horizon, then approximate tail."
- **Latest prefix-horizon amortization gate:** 9B `gen32` code/reason/schema suite says `prefix=11` is a recovery tool, not a default route. Code and reason still had one reject under baseline and prefix11 (`93.94%` and `88.57%` accept respectively) and prefix11 worsened overlap; schema improved from one reject to clean (`88.57% -> 100%`, `overlap_ms 3536.865 -> 2370.218`), but `refresh=1` was still faster on that row (`2085.620`). Focused schema `gen64` then showed baseline already clean (`100%` accept), prefix11 slightly improved self-spec overlap (`4430.908 -> 4213.992`), and refresh1 regressed (`4544.200`); all rows remained slower than plain exact (`plain_speedup < 1`). Verdict: exact-refresh prefix can rescue some early-drift regimes, but it is not the breakthrough. Next speed path should either predict when to skip self-spec/prefix entirely, or reduce proposal body cost; more exact refresh just moves work from low-rank draft back toward exact decode.
- **Latest cheap-body blockpred gate:** existing `lowrank-ffn-blockpred-P` and `blocktop-10` probes were rechecked on 9B `layers=0,2`, rank64, `gen16`, schedule `4`, code/reason/schema. This refutes immediate sparse-FFN kernel work for the current selector: code stayed clean for all variants but `pca-updown16` was the only small draft-ms improvement; reason regressed from clean baseline to one reject (`78.95%` accept) for `blocktop`, `blockpred`, and `pca-updown`; schema kept the same one reject as baseline and all cheap variants were slower in the dense wall probe. Verdict: predicted block sparsity remains an interesting quality objective, but current nearest-neighbor selector does not improve acceptance or measured draft cost enough to justify Metal sparse FFN fusion. Next cheap-body branch should focus on a real pre-FFN learned/linear block selector or revisit pca-updown on broader layer maps, not dense blockpred.
- **Latest update-risk bridge gate:** added default-off `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-update-risk-threshold=F` to preplan Metal exact-refresh offsets from the CPU teacher-forced fallback score without per-token GPU readback. This validates the bridge interface but refutes token-level max `update=max(beta*residual)` as a speed selector: on the schema `gen16` gate, thresholds from `0.35` to `1.0` move from `14/16` refresh offsets to `0/16`, with parity preserved after switching the planner to the routed `forward_top1` exact oracle, but useful sparse offsets do not beat the already-clean baseline within noisy wall timing. Treat this as diagnostic substrate for per-layer/head or prefix-horizon selectors, not a default route.
- **Latest layer-specific update-risk gate:** added default-off `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-draft-update-risk-layer-threshold=F`. It can exact-refresh only the risky selected low-rank layer(s) at an offset instead of all selected low-rank layers. This is a real granularity improvement, but the first gates refute it as the missing speed/quality route: schema stays clean with sparse layer refreshes, but the clean baseline is faster within noise; code still rejects even when `t=0.6` refreshes layer `0` at the final generated offset. Conclusion: `update` is an exact-work cost diagnostic, not a candidate-correctness predictor. Next branch should use route-quality signals or cheaper proposal bodies, not more scalar update thresholds.
- **Latest proposal-quality pivot:** self top2/tree2 and MTP K2 were checked after `update` risk failed as a correctness predictor. A 9B code reject was not rescued by `tree2_first`, and `tree2_anywhere` diagnosed it as a self-top2 miss. The 27B MTP/self-draft fusion oracle then gave the strongest current proposal-quality signal: on code stress `gen24`, self top2 resolved `15/24`, but MTP K2 only on self-top2 misses resolved `23/24` with `9` MTP calls and `8` rescues. Treat this as the next implementation target: mismatch-only MTP K2 controller/suite, not always-on MTP and not more scalar threshold tuning.
- **Latest real-boundary tree/MTP gate:** real JSON `gen32/gamma4` says self-top2 is the bigger immediate lever: baseline had `5` rejects; only `1` was a self-top2 miss, and warmed MTP K2 rescued that one in `16.485ms` after pre-decode warmup. Existing `tree2_anywhere` rescued the other `4` rejects and cut overlap to `4713.656ms`, but it verifies accepted tokens serially; `tree2_staged=2` was slower (`6253.702ms`). Next speed branch: branch-state/chunk-major self-top2 verifier first, warmed/resident MTP second.
- **Latest branch-guard verifier primitive:** added default-off `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-tree2-branch-guard=F`. Unlike the refuted margin guard, this path verifies only the accepted prefix before the first low-margin token; if the guard token rejects, the verifier is still at the exact pre-token state, so resync can copy that live branch state and advance the exact correction without restore+replay. Verification: release build and top2 spec passed. A hostile 9B smoke (`rank8/layers=0,2,4/draft_skip_recurrent_ffn/gen8/guard=10`) preserved `parity=true`, forced `8` rejects, and recorded `tree2_branch_guard_replayless_resyncs=7` with `replay_ms=0.0`, proving the primitive. On a 27B JSON-ish `gen32/gamma4` gate, `guard=2.0` preserved parity and hit `4` pass-only guards but did not catch the one real reject; `guard=10.0` over-triggered pass-only guards and delayed draft work. Verdict: branch-state resync works, but raw margin threshold is not enough as the selector. Next speed work should keep this primitive and search for a better guard signal (reject-risk features, position/history, top2 gap plus route context), not simply raise the threshold. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_branch_guard_hostile_minimal_20260506151725.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_branch_guard_json_baseline_20260506150800.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_branch_guard_json_guard2_20260506150930.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_branch_guard_json_guard10_20260506151054.log`.
- **Latest self-spec router trace gate:** added default-off `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-router-trace=PATH`, a JSONL trace of self-draft router/reject-risk features with no raw token ids. It records chunk/index, generated offset, verifier tail skip, draft margin/proposal min margin, draft top1/top2 hit, guard role, risk-offramp, next-pre-submit, draft-updown, and prior accept/reject counters. Verification passed with release build, help flag, and top2 spec (`2 examples, 0 failures`). Evidence: 9B hostile no-FFN smoke produced 8 reject rows and parity true; 9B normal lowrank produced 16 accepted rows with no rejects; 27B JSON-ish `gen32/gamma4` produced one real reject at offset 13 with margin `0.959`, while a clean accepted token had lower margin `0.475`. Threshold `1.0` catches the reject but has 4 accept false positives, and `tree2_branch_guard=1.0` had `4` pass-only branch-guard hits, `0` replayless resyncs, and the reject in `branch_suffix`. Decision: raw margin is an early-warning feature, not a branch point. Next implement/test a guarded-prefix snapshot route: when a guard fires and passes, keep an exact state snapshot at that boundary so a later suffix reject can replay/resync from the guard boundary instead of the chunk start. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_router_trace_hostile_20260506152653.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_router_trace_lowrank_20260506152724.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_router_trace_json_20260506152804.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_router_trace_json_branch1_20260506152955.log`.
- [x] Implement guarded-prefix snapshot/resync for pass-guard suffix rejects as a default-off branch-state experiment. Added `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-tree2-branch-guard-snapshot`, preserving parity and reporting snapshot copy/suffix replay counters. The primitive works but the naive full-copy form is refuted as a speed path: 9B hostile guard-token rejects remain replayless (`replay_ms=0`, `tree2_branch_guard_replayless_resyncs=7`), but 27B JSON-ish `guard=1.0` paid `3` snapshots (`62.429ms`) plus split-verifier overhead and regressed overlap (`3751.291ms` no-snapshot -> `5315.587ms` snapshot) while saving only a one-token suffix replay (`78.813ms`). `guard=0.5` reduced copies to `1` (`34.149ms`) but produced no suffix replay saving in that run. Next version, if pursued, must avoid full recurrent-state copies or expose cheap in-verifier checkpoints; do not tune thresholds around this implementation. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_branch_snapshot_hostile_20260506153829.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_branch_snapshot_json_nosnapshot_20260506153858.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_branch_snapshot_json_snapshot_20260506153858.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_branch_snapshot_json_guard05_nosnapshot_20260506154153.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_branch_snapshot_json_guard05_snapshot_20260506154153.log`.
- [x] Remove allocation/prepare noise from branch-guard snapshots with a reusable scratch state. This keeps the snapshot branch default-off but makes the counter more honest: 27B JSON-ish `guard=0.5` snapshot copy dropped from `34.149ms` to `2.865ms`, while `guard=1.0` measured `46.473ms` across `3` snapshots and was near the prior no-snapshot wall reference in that noisy run. 9B hostile replayless guard-token reject still preserved parity. Verdict: preallocation is a useful cleanup, but it does not make branch guards a speed path; false-positive guards and extra guard/suffix verifier splits remain the problem. Logs: `/tmp/qwen36_branch_snapshot_reuse_json_guard10_20260506154754.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_branch_snapshot_reuse_json_guard05_20260506154754.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_branch_snapshot_reuse_hostile_20260506155031.log`.
- [x] Add `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-tree2-branch-guard-until-reject`, a default-off router refinement that disables branch-guard scans after the first real reject in the run. This targets the observed 27B JSON-ish pattern where the first `guard=1.0` warning was useful but later low-margin guards after one correction were false positives. Verification passed with release build, help flag, and top2 spec. The 27B JSON-ish same-binary A/B preserved parity and reduced structural waste: branch-guard hits `4 -> 1`, snapshot copies `3 -> 1`, snapshot copy time `90.468ms -> 34.325ms`, while keeping the one useful suffix replay. It is not promoted as a wall-clock speed claim because full-state copy and verifier wall are noisy/dominant; treat it as evidence for history-aware routing. - [x] Add `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-tree2-branch-guard-snapshot-min-prefix=N`, a default-preserving expected-value filter for guarded-prefix snapshots. The default `N=1` keeps prior behavior; `N=2` skips snapshots that can only save one accepted prefix token. On the 27B JSON-ish `guard=1.0/snapshot/until-reject` gate, `min_prefix=2` preserved parity and avoided the short-prefix full-state copy entirely (`snapshot_copies=0`, `snapshot_ms=0`, `suffix_replays=0`) while falling back to normal backup replay (`replay_ms=142.491`). - [x] Add `bin/qwen35_self_spec_router_trace_score.cr`, an offline expected-value scorer for `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-router-trace` JSONL files. Re-scoring the 27B JSON-ish trace reproduces the current branch-guard economics: threshold `1.0` sees `4/9` candidate chunks but only `1` suffix reject, `3` snapshot copies at `min_prefix=1`, and only `1` saved prefix token; with `snapshot_cost_ms=34` and `replay_token_ms=80`, even the best short-prefix row is still negative (`net_ms=-22`). This validates expected-value scoring as the next router tool before more runtime policies. Runtime A/B on the structured JSON row confirmed the replayless route: `branch_guard=1.0` preserved parity, turned `replay_ms 375.770 -> 0.0`, and moved overlap `5523.084 -> 5170.893` while still below plain exact (`plain_speedup 0.3958x -> 0.4221x`). The scorer found a conditional snapshot-positive pattern: threshold `1.0` + `min_prefix=3` had `3` candidates, `2` useful snapshots, `1` wasted snapshot, `6` saved prefix tokens, and rough `net_ms=378`; threshold `4.0` + `min_prefix=3` had `1` useful snapshot and `0` wasted snapshots. Focused A/B confirmed structural replay reduction with exact parity: `structured_sql` replay `186.530 -> 64.988ms` with one `2.779ms` snapshot, and `reason_bench` replay `180.865 -> 64.220ms` with one `3.020ms` snapshot. Wall improved on both single runs, but paired exact baselines were noisy, so this is not a default policy yet. Decision: guarded-prefix snapshots are useful only under expected-value routing for long-prefix suffix rejects; raw margin alone is insufficient. - [x] Source-audit Qwen3.6/vLLM MTP before further MTP tuning. Qwen3.6 local HF config says `model_type=qwen3_5`, `mtp_num_hidden_layers=1`, and `mtp_use_dedicated_embeddings=false`; the README recommends server-side NEXTN/MTP, and local vLLM remaps Qwen MTP to a same-model draft path. Our formula path matches the MTP shape and one-token shortcut, so the issue is not "wrong weights"; the issue is execution model: our current wall is dominated by verifier/scheduler/replay, not by MTP proposal quality. Next MTP work should be resident/fused and paired with a chunk-major verifier, or kept as sparse K2 fallback after self-top2/branch-state failure.

Corrected 27B runs used `guard=1.0`, `min_prefix=3`, `suffix_threshold=2.0`, rank8 early6 layers, fixed `gamma=4`. Results: `gen12` six-prompt sweep and `gen32` focused YAML/SQL/reason sweep preserved parity on all `27/27` corrected rows. On suffix-positive rows, `onepass_suffix2` kept replay reduction and usually reduced verifier chunks versus split: `structured_sql gen32` overlap `6154.782 -> 5089.917 -> 4656.204ms` (`nosnap -> split -> onepass`, chunks `9 -> 10 -> 8`); `reason_bench gen32` `4997.003 -> 4384.807 -> 4222.023ms`. Gen12 was positive on SQL (`1622.053 -> 1405.836 -> 1389.492ms`) but mixed on reason (`1659.981 -> 1413.994 -> 1535.869ms`), so one-pass is a promising suffix-row primitive, not a universal default. A reverse-order `structured_yaml gen32` control showed the earlier huge pass-clean regression was order/load variance, not branch-split semantics: reverse rows were near-neutral (`onepass 3351.713`, `split 3390.677`, `nosnap 3377.321ms`). Logs: `/tmp/qwen36_branch_mode_sweep_20260506233125/gen12_corrected.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_branch_mode_sweep_20260506233125/gen32_corrected.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_branch_mode_sweep_20260506233125/gen32_yaml_reverse.log`, summaries in the same directory.
- [x] Run duplicated-mode ABBA in one process on the 27B suffix-positive prompts. Command order was `nosnap,split_suffix2,onepass_suffix2,onepass_suffix2,split_suffix2,nosnap`, `gen32/gamma4`, `structured_sql` main plus `reason_bench` suite. All `12/12` rows preserved parity. Median overlap: `structured_sql` `nosnap 3784.812ms`, `split 3504.915ms`, `onepass 3482.126ms`; `reason_bench` `3952.458ms`, `3617.072ms`, `3480.521ms`. Median replay dropped from `~187-191ms` to `~61-64ms`; one-pass reduced verifier chunks versus split (`10 -> 8`) and had the best median on both prompts. This supports keeping one-pass suffix checkpoint as the leading branch-state speed path, but n=2/prompt is still not enough for default promotion. Logs: `/tmp/qwen36_branch_mode_sweep_20260506233125/gen32_suffix_positive_abba.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_branch_mode_sweep_20260506233125/gen32_suffix_positive_abba_summary.md`.
- [x] Run explicit 27B promotion gate for one-pass suffix checkpoints over `5` repeats on suffix-positive and pass-clean prompts. Gate used `gen32/gamma4`, rank8 early6, `guard=1.0`, `min_prefix=3`, `suffix_threshold=2.0`, four prompts (`structured_sql`, `reason_bench`, `structured_yaml`, `math_matrix`), and modes `nosnap/split_suffix2/onepass_suffix2` in one process. All `60/60` rows preserved exact parity. `onepass_suffix2` improved median overlap versus `nosnap` on all prompts (`reason_bench -348.615ms`, `structured_sql -628.574ms`, `structured_yaml -39.268ms`, `math_matrix -119.456ms`) and had the best aggregate median delta, but it did not beat `split_suffix2` on every prompt (`structured_sql` was `+5.341ms` slower than split, `math_matrix` `+47.482ms` slower). Verdict: do not promote a blind global default; keep one-pass as the leading suffix-checkpoint primitive behind a gated/router policy. Logs: `/tmp/qwen36_branch_mode_promotion_20260507071352/promotion_gate.log`, summary: `/tmp/qwen36_branch_mode_promotion_20260507071352/promotion_summary.md`.
- [x] Add an opt-in branch snapshot mode policy/router for calibrated suffix-checkpoint runs. New `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-branch-snapshot-policy=MODE` applies named policies without changing defaults: `off`, `split`, `onepass`, `split_min3_suffix2`, and `onepass_min3_suffix2`. The calibrated `onepass_min3_suffix2` policy sets snapshot mode, single-pass checkpoint, snapshot-only split, `min_prefix=3`, and `suffix_threshold=2.0`, so the runtime uses one-pass only for suffix-value-positive branch chunks and otherwise avoids the no-value split. - [x] Make calibrated branch snapshot modes first-class in the in-process mode sweep. `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-branch-snapshot-modes=LIST` now accepts `split_min3_suffix2` and `onepass_min3_suffix2`, and applies `min_prefix=3,suffix_threshold=2.0` per row instead of relying on separate global threshold flags. - [x] Run calibrated 27B mode gate with self-contained `split_min3_suffix2` / `onepass_min3_suffix2` modes. It also exposed a router flaw: on `structured_yaml`, suffix-gated modes disabled branch guard entirely (`hits=0`) and lost nosnap's replayless guard-token resync (`nosnap median 4356.646ms`, onepass-only-split `4843.898ms`). Combined scoring over the focused + screen + negative traces (`82` chunks) shows the best current offline split at `threshold=0.5,no_snapshot_threshold=0.2,min_prefix=3,suffix<=2.0`: `4` candidates, `2` guard rejects, `1` useful snapshot, `0` pass-clean, versus `no_snapshot_threshold=0.5` with `12` candidates and `8` pass-clean false positives. This is feature/oracle evidence only; it does not claim runtime wall speed. - [x] Run a real 27B runtime A/B for the scorer-selected policy. Gate used `tree2_branch_guard=0.5`, `gen32/gamma4`, rank8 early6, five prompts, duplicated mode order, and preserved parity on all `40/40` rows. `onepass_min3_suffix2_guard02` improved median overlap versus `nosnap` on `4/5` prompts and aggregate median delta was `-311.704ms`, but it regressed `repeat_markdown_tbl` by `+391.974ms`. Plain `onepass_min3_suffix2` had the best aggregate median delta (`-427.858ms`) and won suffix SQL strongly (`-2238.315ms`), but regressed `repeat_sql_values` by `+806.090ms`. `keepguard` still regressed two pass-clean repeat prompts. Verdict: no runtime promotion yet. - [x] Attribute the branch-policy verifier-wall contradiction before adding another scalar gate. Added a cost-truth row for the actual no-snapshot branch-guard runtime shape (`prefix + guard_suffix`), because the old `--simulate-cost-truth-branch-splits` only measured the stronger `prefix + guard + suffix` split. On Qwen3.6-27B/repeat-markdown `k4,g2`, whole verifier was `175.851ms`; old 3-way split was slower (`216.139ms`, `1.229x`), and actual no-snapshot 2-way split was also slower (`195.253ms`, `1.110x`), all with `match=true`. A focused ABBA rerun showed the previous markdown regression was not stable (`band 5072.746ms` vs `nosnap 5883.140ms` median overlap), but a broader 3-prompt re-gate remained mixed: band won the SQL-like main prompt by `-1419.355ms`, regressed markdown by `+186.043ms`, and regressed repeat SQL-values by `+477.562ms`, with parity true throughout. `QWEN35_HEAD_TOP1_ROWS_MIN=4` was tested as an env-only verifier-head idea and refuted (`k4` verifier worsened `175.851 -> 208.110ms`). Verdict: no branch-policy promotion; the remaining issue is not split count alone but noisy verifier/draft scheduling and the fact self-spec rows are still often slower than `plain_exact_ms`. Logs: `/tmp/qwen36_cost_truth_nosnap_split_20260507195226.log`, `/tmp/qwen36_band_markdown_abba_verify_20260507195330/summary.tsv`, `/tmp/qwen36_band_broader_regate_20260507195651/summary.tsv`, `/tmp/qwen36_cost_truth_head_rows_min4_20260507200623.log`.
- [x] Next speed gate: stop optimizing branch routing in isolation and attack the persistent `plain_speedup<1` cases. First pass compared `plain_exact_ms` vs self-spec phase counters on no-value/pass-clean and reject rows. The key finding is that long-gamma low-rank self-draft can be profitable only on very clean/repetitive spans, while reject-prone or even clean rank4 rows are still dominated by proposal-body wall. 27B repeat-markdown `rank8/gamma32` was near plain (`plain_speedup=0.9543`), `rank4/gamma32` crossed plain in one focused run (`1.0543`), and `rank2/gamma32` crossed more strongly on the same repeat prompt (`1.333x` in the 3-prompt gate), but rank2/rank4 failed broader prompt stability. Rank2 3-prompt gate: main `0.5144x` with one reject, repeat-markdown `1.333x` clean, repeat-SQL-values `0.406x` with one reject. Rank4 3-prompt gate: main `0.4493x` with one reject, repeat-markdown `0.8368x` clean, repeat-SQL-values `0.8398x` clean. A main-prompt rank2 gamma sweep showed gamma tuning alone is insufficient: `gamma4/8/16/32` stayed `0.5342x/0.4738x/0.5026x/0.4050x`, with small gamma paying verifier chunks and large gamma paying wasted/resync draft. `draft_no_ffn` at rank8/gamma32 was refuted on repeat-markdown (`accept_rate=63.27%`, `plain_speedup=0.6129x`). MTP-K2-on-reject diagnostic on the rank2/gamma32 main prompt rescued the only reject (`checks=1,rescues=1`, `22.591ms`), but this is not a causal speedup until an alternate-branch/rescue path avoids low-rank resync or wasted tail. Logs: `/tmp/qwen36_gamma_sweep_passclean_20260507200922`, `/tmp/qwen36_gamma32_passclean_20260507201112`, `/tmp/qwen36_gamma32_noffn_passclean_20260507201251`, `/tmp/qwen36_gamma32_rank4_passclean_20260507201443`, `/tmp/qwen36_gamma32_rank2_passclean_20260507201615`, `/tmp/qwen36_gamma32_rank2_3prompt_20260507201756`, `/tmp/qwen36_gamma32_rank4_3prompt_20260507202122`, `/tmp/qwen36_main_rank2_gamma_sweep_20260507202455`, `/tmp/qwen36_main_rank2_gamma32_mtp_reject_diag_20260507202952`.
- [x] Add a causal fail-closed self-spec reject off-ramp probe, `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-reject-offramp-after=N`. It preserves parity and stops drafting after N rejects, then finishes the requested tokens with exact greedy decode. First 27B main/rank2/gamma32 A/B refutes it as the standalone speed path: it removed low-rank resync (`attr_draft_resync_ms=0`) but exact suffix fallback cost `3498.038ms`; absolute overlap worsened (`5649.027ms baseline -> 7013.817ms off-ramp`). Verdict: off-ramping after the bad long proposal is too late. The next causal branch must either stop proposal generation before risky tails or have an alternate branch ready. Build/spec evidence: `/tmp/qwen35_reject_offramp_probe_min`, focused specs `4 examples, 0 failures`; current-source smoke `/tmp/qwen35_reject_offramp_smoke_20260507.log`; benchmark log `/tmp/qwen36_main_rank2_gamma32_reject_offramp_ab_20260507203754/summary.tsv`.
- [x] Refute two simple follow-up knobs for the causal high-accept route. Existing progressive schedule `4,4,8,16` avoids long first tails but loses on verifier chunk count: 27B rank2 3-prompt suite measured main `0.5168x`, repeat-markdown `0.5765x`, repeat-SQL-values `0.4897x`, all parity true. Rank8/gamma32 is not a robustness fix either: main still rejected and measured `0.4286x`, while clean repeat rows stayed slower than plain (`0.7765x`, `0.8478x`) because draft body cost dominates. Prompt residual features are the first useful pre-submit signal: at rank2, clean repeat-markdown has lower residual mean / higher pass@0.5 (`0.514299`, `50.94%`) than main (`0.597178`, `29.12%`) and repeat-SQL-values (`0.573695`, `36.04%`). This suggests a conservative residual-threshold router, not more gamma/rank tuning. Logs: `/tmp/qwen36_rank2_progressive_schedule_3prompt_20260507204641/summary.tsv`, `/tmp/qwen36_rank8_gamma32_3prompt_20260507204939/summary.tsv`, `/tmp/qwen36_route_features_rank2_rank8_20260507205310/features.tsv`.
- [x] Implement the prompt/span residual router as a default-off probe. Added `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-residual-router-mean-max=F`, `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-residual-router-pass-threshold=F`, and `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-residual-router-pass-rate-min=F`; the router computes held-out residual stats before fixed-gamma pipeline submit and emits an explicit exact greedy fallback row when the span fails the gate. Build/spec and 9B smoke checks passed (`/tmp/qwen35_residual_router_probe`, `/tmp/qwen35_residual_router_skip_smoke.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_residual_router_run_smoke.log`; focused specs `4 examples, 0 failures`). On the 27B rank2/gamma32 3-prompt gate with `mean_max=0.53`, `pass@0.5>=45%`, it selected exactly the previously clean repeat-markdown row and skipped the two fragile rows with parity true: main `skip/1.0x`, repeat-markdown `run/0.7341x` in this run, repeat-SQL `skip/1.0x`. Verdict: mechanics verified, but no promotion; residual predicts acceptance risk better than wall speed. Log: `/tmp/qwen36_residual_router_gate_20260507210804/summary.tsv`.
- [x] Add a second pre-submit value signal for residual-routed high-gamma self-spec. New default-off flags `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-value-repeat-rate-min=F`, `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-value-bigram-repeat-rate-min=F`, and `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-value-unique-rate-max=F` compute held-out prompt repetition/entropy stats before submitting GPU self-spec and combine fail-closed with the residual router. Build/spec and 9B skip/run smokes passed (`/tmp/qwen35_value_router_probe`, `/tmp/qwen35_value_router_skip_smoke.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_value_router_run_smoke.log`; focused specs `4 examples, 0 failures`). The first 27B diagnostic gate showed useful separation: main `repeat_rate=31.25`, repeat-markdown `85.94`, repeat-SQL `79.69`; with residual gate plus `repeat_rate_min=0`, repeat-markdown ran clean at `plain_speedup=1.1132x` while main/SQL exact-fell-back. A stricter causal gate `repeat_rate_min=80` selected only repeat-markdown, but that run measured `plain_speedup=0.8052x` under heavy host timing variance. Verdict: value features are useful router inputs and the plumbing is verified, but no promotion; the next gate must use ABBA/repeats or direct draft/verifier lower-bound features to predict wall benefit, not just prompt repetition. Logs: `/tmp/qwen36_value_router_gate_20260507212107/summary.tsv`, `/tmp/qwen36_value_router_repeat80_gate_20260507212503/summary.tsv`.
- [x] Reduce timing-noise dependence in the residual+value high-gamma router and test a direct cost-value falsifier. A 3x repeated 27B gate for residual+value `repeat_rate_min=80` preserved parity on all `9/9` rows and selected only repeat-markdown for self-spec, but the selected clean row stayed slower than plain with stable negative median: `plain_speedup=0.7728/0.8167/0.8085`, median `0.8085x`, `100%` accept. Phase medians show why: selected markdown had `draft_seed_ms=5990.120`, `verifier_ms=1087.475`, `plain_exact_ms=5793.568`, so the draft body alone was at/above plain and verifier was overhead. Cost-truth makes the lower bound explicit: main `draft+verifier_rel=1.7495`, repeat-markdown `1.0948`, repeat-SQL `1.5853`; even markdown's state-only lower bound plus verifier is `1.0079`. Focused WBA on markdown confirmed runtime attribution (`attr_draft_profiled_ms=2121.851`, `attr_verifier_total_ms=597.397`, `plain_exact_ms=2349.898`, `plain_speedup=0.8626x`). Resident `pca-updown16` on the same selected span was refuted as a body-speed rescue (`accept=65.96%`, `plain_speedup=0.5327x`). Verdict: do not promote repetition/residual routing; the next breakthrough needs a materially cheaper proposal source/body, not another scalar router. Logs: `/tmp/qwen36_value_router_repeat80_abba_20260507213431/summary.md`, `/tmp/qwen36_value_router_cost_truth_more_20260507215058/summary.md`, `/tmp/qwen36_value_router_markdown_attr_20260507215412/summary.tsv`, `/tmp/qwen36_value_router_markdown_updown_ab_20260507215800/summary.tsv`.
- [x] Next speed gate: attack proposal cost directly. Current exact self-spec only wins if `draft_body + verifier < plain`; for the tested 27B high-gamma route this is false even on the clean repetitive span. Candidate falsifiers: near-free exact-verified prompt-copy/repetition proposals for repetitive spans, more aggressive same-weight body skipping with a pre-submit acceptance probe, latent/shortlist draft head to cut LM-head/top1 cost, or a fused draft body that removes enough weight traffic to push markdown's `draft+verifier_rel` below `0.9`.

Remote RTX 5060 Ti all-layer Qwen3.5-9B valid start9 gate keeps active verification enabled and accepts `12/12` at `146.421ms` (`12.202ms/tok`). Invalid start8 gate disables active verification, proposes `0` tokens, and runs near plain fallback (`282.213ms` vs prefix plain `277.427ms`) instead of the previous active-band gated-out overhead (`323.267ms`).
      - [x] Fix cursor advancement after serial fallback inside cursor-only active replay. When a risk gate or empty proposal forces one exact serial token, the controller now advances the source cursor if that exact token matches the source token at the cursor; otherwise it drops the cursor. This prevents a stale source cursor from causing a false reject after a fallback. Remote RTX 5060 Ti all-layer Qwen3.5-9B gen64 cache replay with risk gate on now has no rejects, verifies `62/62` active tokens plus two serial cursor advances, and runs at `891.908ms` (`13.936ms/tok`), beating same-host llama.cpp tg64 `14.89ms/tok`. With the risk gate disabled for a validated trusted cursor, the same gen64 path accepts `64/64` in chunks `4,4,8,16,16,16` at `730.050ms` (`11.407ms/tok`).
      - [x] Make the trusted-cache fast lane explicit and fail-closed. `--greedy-loop-probe-ngram-trusted-source` bypasses the risky-shape gate only after source/prefix cursor validation passes; invalid cursors keep `trusted_source_active=false`, disable active verification before allocation, and fall back exactly. Remote RTX 5060 Ti all-layer Qwen3.5-9B valid gen64 trusted-source replay accepts `64/64` with chunks `4,4,8,16,16,16` at `738.944ms` (`11.546ms/tok`) while keeping `chunk_probe_ngram_risk_gate=true`; invalid start8 trusted-source replay proposes `0` tokens, disables active verification, and runs near plain fallback at `283.719ms` (`23.643ms/tok`).
      - [x] Lift trusted-cache replay to bulk WBA bands and fix a fail-closed validation edge. For artifact-level trusted cursors, larger active bands reduce controller overhead without changing exact verification: remote RTX 5060 Ti all-layer Qwen3.5-9B gen64 accepted `64/64` with schedule `32,32` at `704.280ms` (`11.004ms/tok`) and with schedule `64` at `698.010ms` (`10.906ms/tok`), versus the prior `4,4,8,16,16,16` trusted run at `738.944ms` (`11.546ms/tok`). Adversary found that an invalid prefix gate could disable active verification before allocation but still hit the `--tokens=1` validation guard when callers omitted `--tokens 1`; the probe now forces `tokens=1` in that fail-closed path. Remote invalid start8/gamma64 without explicit `--tokens 1` now prints `tokens=1`, `chunk_probe_raw_tokens=0`, `chunk_probe_active_verify=false`, and completes at `292.482ms` (`24.373ms/tok`) with `ok=true`.
      - [x] Add active-verifier snapshot/restore attribution for CUDA cursor replay. Remote RTX 5060 Ti all-layer Qwen3.5-9B valid trusted bulk replay (`gen64`, schedule `64`) measured `chunk_probe_active_snapshot_ms=2.587`, `chunk_probe_active_verify_ms=692.239`, `chunk_probe_active_restore_ms=0.0`, and `cuda_ms=699.036` (`10.922ms/tok`). Invalid prefix-gated replay now reports zero active snapshot/verify/restore work. A deliberately corrupted same-prefix source triggered one active reject with snapshot `3.309ms`, verify `141.325ms`, restore `0.418ms`, and exact fallback `ok=true`. Verdict: rollback copy is visible but not the current wall on clean trusted replay; the dominant cost remains the exact active WBA verifier body.
      - [x] Make `--profile-phases` work for `--known-replay-active-schedule-run` and profile the exact bulk verifier body. Remote RTX 5060 Ti all-layer Qwen3.5-9B known replay schedule `64` preserved `64/64` acceptance at `696.057ms` (`10.876ms/tok`) with phase attribution. Recurrent layers cost `~19.3-20.4ms` each; full-attention layers cost `~15.8-16.8ms` each; output head costs `76.479ms` with `69.882ms` in logits and `5.850ms` in top1. A representative recurrent layer spends `~3.8ms` projection, `~4.5-4.6ms` recurrent core, and `~11.7-11.9ms` FFN. Verdict: next exact kernel work should target recurrent FFN/head or a stronger cache artifact that restores future state; rollback elimination is not the main lever.
      - [x] Add a trusted cache-artifact restore lower-bound probe. `--known-replay-trusted-artifact-restore` simulates an artifact that already stores the exact token span plus post-span decode state for the same model/source contract: it builds/snapshots that future state outside the timed region, poisons/resets the runner, then times only state restore and emits cached token ids as the replay output. Remote RTX 5060 Ti all-layer Qwen3.5-9B start1/gen64 reports `known_replay_policy=trusted_artifact_restore`, `known_replay_accepted=64`, `ok=true`, excluded artifact build `614.443ms`, snapshot `2.630ms`, poison `32.988ms`, and timed restore `0.189ms` (`0.003ms/token`). Non-zero start9/gen24 also works (`restore=0.198ms`, `ok=true`). This is not generation and not verifier replay; it is the product lower bound for a validated session/hash/state artifact.
      - [x] Measure a host-backed trusted cache-artifact restore upper bound. `--known-replay-trusted-artifact-host-restore` snapshots the post-span decode state into host `Bytes`, poisons/resets the runner, then times host-to-device restore while emitting the cached token ids. Remote RTX 5060 Ti all-layer Qwen3.5-9B start1/gen64 with `max_seq=128` reports `known_replay_trusted_artifact_bytes=61079552`, snapshot `37.542ms`, timed restore `12.259ms` (`0.192ms/token`), `known_replay_accepted=64`, and `ok=true`. Non-zero start9/gen24 also works with the same full-cache artifact size and restore `12.331ms`. This is a conservative full-cache H2D bound, not durable artifact IO; live-length KV snapshots and disk/hash timing remain separate production work.
      - [x] Add and measure live-KV host artifacts. `--known-replay-trusted-artifact-live-kv` stores only full-attention KV rows up to the restored cursor while preserving full recurrent state. A full-cache host restore smoke still works (`61079552` bytes, `12.257ms`, `ok=true`). Verdict: KV trimming helps but only modestly at short context because recurrent DeltaNet state dominates the artifact size.
      - [x] Measure persistent-path artifact IO and host hash timing. `--known-replay-trusted-artifact-io-probe` writes the host snapshot to a file, hashes it through the host `sha256sum`/`shasum` tool, reads it back into a fresh host snapshot, and restores that read snapshot. Start9/gen24 live-KV artifact (`54788096` bytes = `52690944` recurrent + `2097152` KV) reports `18.249/42.994/53.814/12.825ms`, `24/24`, `ok=true`. Verdict: persistent IO/hash dominates H2D restore unless production uses memory-mapped/pinned artifacts, async prefetch, weaker staged hashes, or overlap.
      - [x] Test contiguous host artifact read reconstruction. `--known-replay-trusted-artifact-contiguous-read` reads the artifact into one host allocation and reconstructs the snapshot from slices before H2D restore. Start9/gen24 preserved `24/24`, `ok=true`, but read stayed noisy/slower (`43.618ms`). Verdict: fragmented reads/allocation have some cost, but not enough; product replay still needs prefetch/resident artifacts and recurrent-state compression.
      - [x] Add first recurrent-state codec diagnostic. `--known-replay-trusted-artifact-codec-bench` runs a scalar CPU block-INT8 encode/decode error benchmark over recurrent host buffers only. Block sweep: block64 `0.2656x`, RMSE `0.008798`, encode/decode `469.240/124.608ms`; block1024 `0.2510x`, RMSE `0.015476`, `458.173/121.891ms`; block4096 `0.2502x`, RMSE `0.019087`, `457.235/121.647ms`. Verdict: recurrent state is quantizable to ~4x smaller, but scalar CPU codec is far too slow and compressed restore/parity is not yet proven.
      - [x] Add the recurrent codec restore/parity gate. `--known-replay-trusted-artifact-codec-restore-check` now decodes block-INT8 recurrent buffers into a host snapshot, restores the decoded state, and compares one continuation top1 against an exact uncompressed restored state. Remote RTX 5060 Ti Qwen3.5-9B start9/gen24 passed block64/256/1024/4096 (`decoded_top1=13`, `exact_top1=13`, `ok=true`); later cursor start33/gen8 also passed block256 and block4096.       - [x] Add source-aligned multi-step recurrent codec continuation parity. `--known-replay-trusted-artifact-codec-restore-steps=N` now replays N known future input tokens from both exact-restored and decoded-INT8-restored states, reporting input/expected/exact/decoded top1 sequences plus parity/source counts.       - [x] Add free-run greedy recurrent codec parity. `--known-replay-trusted-artifact-codec-free-run-steps=N` now restores exact and decoded-INT8 states, starts from the same post-artifact input token, feeds each path's own generated top1 back into the next step, and reports exact/decoded free-run sequences plus parity counts. Verdict: the compression branch has now passed source-aligned and self-fed gates on this history, but prompt diversity and scalar codec cost remain blockers.
      - [x] Run a wider same-history free-run codec matrix and refute block4096 as a trusted restore format. Block1024 also passed all six. Block4096 passed earlier cursors but failed later ones: `start25/16` and `start33/8` got `15/16`, while `start41/8` got `6/16`. Verdict: use block1024 or block256 for trusted artifact work; block4096 is proposal-only unless future acceptance gates prove it useful.
      - [x] Add the recurrent codec GPU decode restore path for block1024/block256. `--known-replay-trusted-artifact-codec-gpu-decode-check` keeps recurrent state compressed on host, copies quant/scales to GPU, decodes directly into recurrent CUDA state buffers, restores live KV from host, and uses that decoded state for continuation/free-run parity. Late cursor start41/tokens8 block1024 also passed (`11.489ms -> 3.224ms`), and block256 passed start25/tokens16 (`11.626ms -> 3.333ms`). Verdict: GPU decode turns the recurrent codec from a scalar CPU dead end into a real host-artifact restore win; production promotion still needs broader prompt/history coverage and persistent artifact service design.

Seeds `0/198/760` passed block1024 and block256. Seed `1919` refuted block1024: one-token/source parity still passed, but free-run diverged at step 9 (`free_run_parity_count=8`, `ok=false`) while block256 passed `16/16`. Verdict: use block256 as the trusted compressed recurrent restore candidate; block1024 joins block4096 as proposal-only until an exact verifier gate or adaptive codec proves it safe.
      - [x] Run the first multi-cursor block256 GPU-restore falsifier and refute universal trusted recurrent compression. Four generated 65-token histories (`seed=0,198,760,1919`) were tested across six cursors (`1/16`, `9/16`, `17/16`, `25/16`, `33/8`, `41/8`). Block256 passed `23/24` gates with `16/16` free-run parity and `~2.8-4.0ms` GPU codec restores, but failed `seed=1919,start=1,tokens=16` immediately (`next_exact_top1=5435`, `next_decoded_top1=16661`, `free_run_parity_count=0`, `ok=false`). Retesting the failing cursor with block64/128/256 all produced the same wrong decoded top1, so the issue is early-state quantization sensitivity, not only block size. Verdict: compressed recurrent artifacts must be cursor-age/risk gated; early cache artifacts need raw recurrent state, a stronger codec, or proposal-only exact verification.
      - [x] Add and validate a cursor-age adaptive recurrent artifact restore policy. `--known-replay-trusted-artifact-codec-min-start=N` now keeps early cursors on raw host recurrent restore and uses GPU block-INT8 decode only at cursor `N` and later, while reporting `known_replay_trusted_artifact_codec_restore_policy`. Remote RTX 5060 Ti Qwen3.5-9B with `min_start=9`, block256, four generated histories (`seed=0,198,760,1919`), and six cursors each passed `24/24`: `raw_host_before_min_start` handled all four `start=1` cases, and `gpu_block_i8` handled the remaining 20 cases with restore `~2.95-3.81ms`. A raw prefix of 12 recurrent layers fixed the immediate next token but only reached `3/16` free-run parity. A raw prefix of 18 recurrent layers finally passed `16/16`, but restore was `~11.27ms`, close to raw host restore. Local M2 Max Qwen3.5-9B product-shaped BF16 fast-forward at `max_seq=256`, `gen=16`, no resident state improved p50 restore from `10.1ms` full-KV to `8.0ms` live-KV while preserving cached output ids. Product default remains full-KV/raw unless the live-KV env/flag is explicit.
      - [x] Broaden product-shaped live-KV restore economics across the five-prompt matrix. `scripts/qwen35_cache_artifact_matrix.sh` now supports `QWEN35_MATRIX_LIVE_KV='0 1'` and force rebuilds via `QWEN35_MATRIX_FORCE_BUILD=1`. Local M2 Max Qwen3.5-9B BF16 fast-forward at `max_seq=256`, `gen=16`, `requests=3`, no resident state: full-KV p50 restore was `9.0/9.4/9.4/9.5/9.4ms` for `fact/code/json/repeat/cache`; live-KV p50 restore was `8.0/7.6/7.8/7.2/7.2ms`, winning `5/5`. This supports live-KV as the next default candidate for compressed cold persistent artifacts, pending larger-context/session validation.
      - [x] Fix the encoded-restorer probe's accidental Linux Crystal/OpenSSL 3 link dependency. `Qwen35StateSnapshot.encode_artifact_bytes` and `decode_artifact_encoded_bytes` expose the v2 artifact bytes path without forcing SHA256 codegen; `read_artifact_encoded` now hashes only when `expected_sha256` is supplied. The real hashed `write_artifact` / `read_artifact(expected_sha256: ...)` product path is unchanged. Remote reefy.ai CUDA build now passes without the `EVP_MD_size -> EVP_MD_get_size` shim, and the early BF16 / late block8 INT8 encoded-restorer gates still pass.
      - [x] Add mmap and hash-skip lower-bound probes for persistent trusted artifacts. `--known-replay-trusted-artifact-mmap-read` maps the host artifact and restores from mapped slices; `--known-replay-trusted-artifact-skip-hash` measures session-local/trusted-storage load lower bound without synchronous SHA-256. Remote RTX 5060 Ti all-layer Qwen3.5-9B raw live-KV artifact (`55.84MB`, recurrent `52.69MB`) showed segmented read+restore `30.867+11.558ms`, mmap read+restore `0.031+12.753ms`, and mmap+skip-hash load `12.713ms` vs mmap+SHA load `62.972ms`. Verdict: mmap removes the explicit read copy; synchronous full SHA remains the main load-path bottleneck and should move to validation/prefetch/background policy rather than token-critical restore.
      - [x] Measure and optimize compressed v2 artifact load economics for the encoded CUDA restorer. The first all-layer v2 path exposed a CPU repack bottleneck: late block8 INT8 load was `221.226ms` because restorer construction unpacked interleaved artifact payloads into separate quant/scale arrays (`196.462ms`). Added `recurrent_block_i8_interleaved_decode_f32` and changed `QwenStateArtifactRestorer` to decode interleaved INT8 payloads directly, then removed the extra host payload pack by uploading encoded record payloads by segment. Remote all-layer Qwen3.5-9B seed1919 gates stayed `16/16`, `ok=true`; late INT8 v2 load dropped to `31.210ms` (`read=12.140`, `decode=11.472`, build `1.005`, GPU restore `6.592`) and early BF16 v2 load dropped to `40.209ms`. Remaining bottleneck is now artifact read/decode and many H2D copies, not CPU INT8 repack.
      - [x] Add zero-copy and mmap parsing for encoded v2 artifacts. `decode_artifact_encoded_bytes(copy_payloads: false)` keeps payloads as slices backed by the artifact bytes, and `--known-replay-trusted-artifact-encoded-mmap-read` maps the v2 `.qkv` artifact so encoded-restorer payloads are zero-copy slices of the mapped file. Remote all-layer Qwen3.5-9B seed1919 gates stayed `16/16`, `ok=true`; late block8 INT8 load dropped again from `31.210ms` to `6.783ms` (`read=0.031`, `decode=0.184`, build `0.442`, GPU restore `6.126`), and early BF16 load dropped to `7.795ms`. Remaining token-critical cost is now mostly H2D upload + decode/KV copy, not host artifact parsing.
      - [x] Add GPU-resident preupload for encoded v2 artifacts. `QwenStateArtifactRestorer#preupload_payloads` uploads recurrent encoded payloads and raw KV payloads into CUDA staging buffers before the timed restore; hot restore then decodes recurrent state and copies KV device-to-device. Remote RTX 5060 Ti all-layer Qwen3.5-9B seed1919 gates stayed `16/16`, `ok=true`. With mmap + preupload, BF16 hot restore was `0.366ms` (`kernel=0.236`, `kv=0.129`) versus cold `7.071ms`; late block8 INT8 hot restore was `0.392ms` (`kernel=0.253`, `kv=0.138`) versus cold `6.285ms`. A longer all-layer 512-token history with `start=480`, `max_seq=768` stayed `16/16`, `ok=true`; cold restore was `11.392ms` (`kv=6.276`) while preuploaded hot restore was `0.447ms` (`kernel=0.242`, `kv=0.205`). Cold total remains `~7-14ms` because upload still happens during prefetch; the win is moving it out of the token-critical session-cache activation path.
    - [x] Revisit FFN diamond fusion only at the algebraic boundary (`norm -> gate/up -> SwiGLU -> down/top candidates`) or as a draft-only route. Current ledger says the exact full-FFN diamond is still not a near-term speed path: smaller `gate/up`, `gate/up+SwiGLU`, in-place activation, and naive no-store output-tile streaming branches are already refuted. The live algebraic routes are proposal-only: `pca-updown` has a real integrated layer-kernel win on known spans but loses at current single-token self-draft granularity, while blocktop/blockpred need a better pre-FFN decision-boundary selector before sparse kernels. Do not implement another exact FFN fusion unless it changes the premise to multi-token/layer-resident draft execution or selected-block projection before dense `gate/up`.
      - [x] Recheck late-band `pca-updown` after the current timing fixes. A single 9B ABBA on the default prompt showed a weak positive (`pca-updown32` `+3.24%` median overlap delta, min `+2.47%`, parity/accept clean), but wider gates refute promotion. A global/external FFN adapter over code/json/reason kept parity but dropped acceptance to `77.81%` mean and was `~80%` slower than pure lowrank. FFN reconstruction residual features did not predict the failure (`json` had lower rel-RMSE than `main` but worse acceptance). The same-boundary lowrank-vs-updown agreement gate passed bad routes and added large probe cost. Prompt-local adapters for the same three prompts still rejected on code/json and only kept reason clean while slower. A code-prompt hybrid sweep showed pure lowrank was the only route faster than the hybrid candidates; tail-only updown/no-FFN variants preserved parity but were `~39-59%` slower than pure. Verdict: do not pursue more single-token `pca-updown` super-fusion for the current self-draft lane. Reopen only if the route becomes multi-token/layer-resident enough to amortize training/probe cost, or if a predictor can avoid running the approximate body online. Logs: `/tmp/qwen35_selfspec_updown_abba_9b_20260516212235.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_selfspec_updown_suite_9b_20260516212345.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_ffn_updown_features_9b_20260516212610.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_updown_agreement_suite_9b_20260516212840.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_prompt_local_updown_9b_20260516213630.log`, `/tmp/qwen35_hybrid_routes_code_9b_20260516213910.log`.
- **Last updated:** 2026-05-19

**decision_update_329:** Added atlas-compatible chunk dumps for the real same-model low-rank GPU self-spec pipeline. `qwen35_deltanet_fixed_basis_probe` now accepts `--simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-dump-cycles=PATH` (or `QWEN35_SELF_SPEC_GPU_PIPELINE_DUMP_CYCLES`) and appends one JSONL row per self-draft chunk with `gamma_history`, accepted-prefix counts, reject indices, parity, and proportional timing fields consumable by `qwen35_proposal_block_atlas.cr`.

**evidence_update_329:**
- claim: "The same-model low-rank self-draft pipeline can now be compared in the proposal atlas without collapsing chunks into one misleading aggregate row."
  source: local `CRYSTAL_CACHE_DIR=/tmp/cogni_ml_crystal_cache crystal build --no-codegen bin/qwen35_deltanet_fixed_basis_probe.cr --error-trace` passed; release build with `build/bridge.o` and Metal frameworks passed; smoke `--tokens=48 --calib-tokens=24 --ranks=64 --basis=pca --pca-iters=4 --simulate-logits-rank=64 --simulate-logits-layers=0,2,4 --simulate-generate=8 --simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-gammas=4 --simulate-self-spec-gpu-pipeline-dump-cycles=/tmp/qwen35_selfspec_cycles.jsonl` emitted `chunks=2`, `accepted_draft_tokens=8`, `proposed_tokens=8`, `parity=true`. `crystal run bin/qwen35_proposal_block_atlas.cr -- /tmp/qwen35_selfspec_cycles.jsonl` reported `self_lowrank/gamma=4/self_lowrank`, `accepted=8/8`, `p50_acc=4`, `p90_acc=4`.
  verified_at: 2026-05-19

- claim: "The short dump smoke is an instrumentation proof, not a speed win."
  source: same local smoke reported `plain_exact_ms=199.758`, `overlap_ms=307.608`, `plain_speedup=0.6494x`; atlas `gain_ms=-107.8`.

**quadrumvirate_update_329:**
- cassandra: Chunk-level dump support prevents the earlier aggregate-row measurement trap; a single run-level row would overstate block sizes and hide reject locality.
- daedalus: The next sharper falsifier is a cross-prompt same-model atlas gate that compares low-rank self-draft, n-gram, external draft, and MTP under the same accepted-block/economics table.
- maieutic: Timing fields in chunk rows are proportional allocations from run totals, not per-chunk Metal timestamps. They are valid for source-level economics totals, but not for micro-attribution inside a chunk.
- adversary: Keep `atlas_scope=chunk` and `parity=true` visible in rows; do not promote speed claims from this smoke because it deliberately used a tiny prompt/gen and lost to plain exact decode.

**decision_update_330:** Ran the first cross-prompt same-model low-rank self-draft atlas gates. The mechanism is strong but the current economics are not: low-rank self-draft proposed perfectly accepted blocks across synthetic prompt categories, yet every run remained slower than direct exact decode by `plain_speedup`. This separates "latent block proposal exists" from "proposal body is cheap enough."

**evidence_update_330:**
- claim: "Same-model low-rank self-draft exposes robust accepted-block structure across multiple prompt categories in the current synthetic suite."
  source: local M2 Max release probe `/tmp/qwen35_deltanet_vlbp_dump`, layers `0,2,4`, rank64, PCA basis. `gen=8`, 13 prompts including main plus fact/repeat/code/structured/math/chat suite, gammas `4,8`: 39 atlas chunk rows, gamma4 accepted `104/104`, gamma8 accepted `104/104`, all source runs `parity=true`. `gen=16`, 7 prompts including main plus one prompt per category, gammas `4,8`: 42 atlas chunk rows, gamma4 accepted `112/112`, gamma8 accepted `112/112`, all source runs `parity=true`.
  verified_at: 2026-05-19
  decay_trigger: prompt suite, basis training window, layer set, self-spec scheduler, or model changes
- claim: "Current same-model low-rank self-draft is not faster than plain exact decode in these gates despite 100% acceptance."
  source: `gen=8` summary from `/tmp/qwen35_vlbp_selfspec_suite.log`: gamma4 mean `plain_speedup=0.626` (median `0.6445`, `0/13` positive), gamma8 mean `0.5777` (median `0.5762`, `0/13` positive). `gen=16` summary from `/tmp/qwen35_vlbp_selfspec_suite6_gen16.log`: gamma4 mean `plain_speedup=0.6824` (median `0.6922`, `0/7` positive), gamma8 mean `0.5981` (median `0.5925`, `0/7` positive). Atlas totals: `gen=8` gamma4 gain `-1543.4ms`, gamma8 `-1790.4ms`; `gen=16` all categories had negative gain.

**quadrumvirate_update_330:**
- cassandra: Acceptance is no longer the bottleneck for this specific route; proposal-body and scheduler cost dominate. More gamma tuning without reducing draft body cost is a micro-optimization trap.
- daedalus: The next speed pivot should optimize/eliminate the proposal body, not verifier acceptance: layer-resident super-fusion, cheaper block surrogate, DN summary reuse, or a route that chooses n-gram/MTP/self-lowrank only where its cost model wins.
- maieutic: The repeated synthetic prompts make acceptance easier, so this does not prove broad free-run quality. It does prove that exact verification can anchor multi-token latent proposals when the proposal body tracks the target trajectory.
- adversary: Paired `serial_ms/overlap_ms` speedups look positive in many rows, but the production-relevant comparison is `plain_exact_ms/overlap_ms`; by that metric this branch is currently refuted as a speedup.

**decision_update_331:** Rechecked the self-draft cost split after the cross-prompt atlas. The current early-layer same-model route loses because it is not actually cheap enough: verifier has a faster known-span path in isolation, but the low-rank proposal body still runs too much full-model work, and the pipeline pays per-block orchestration/wait costs. This refutes more gamma-only or broad no-FFN tuning for `layers=0,2,4`.

**evidence_update_331:**
- claim: "Current `layers=0,2,4` low-rank self-draft is proposal-body limited, not acceptance-limited."
  source: local M2 Max attribution run, `gen=16`, gamma4, layers `0,2,4`, rank64: `accepted_draft_tokens=16/16`, `parity=true`, but `plain_speedup=0.6016x`. Cost split: `draft_seed_ms=132.471`, `draft_next_ms=143.973`, `draft_wait_ms=104.476`, `verifier_ms=433.534`, `plain_exact_ms=432.084`; profiled draft wait dominated draft profile (`attr_draft_wait_block_ms=172.776`, `attr_draft_submit_ms=58.479` in the prior attr run).
  verified_at: 2026-05-19
  decay_trigger: self-spec scheduler, layer set, draft kernel fusion, or Metal command-buffer behavior changes
- claim: "Known-span verifier can be faster in isolation, but current pipeline does not turn that into a plain-decode win because the draft body is too expensive."
  source: `--simulate-cost-truth-table=4,8,16`, same prompt/layers/rank: plain exact `22.902ms/tok`; exact known-span verifier `k16=11.071ms/tok`; lowrank state-only known draft `26.331ms/tok`; lowrank GPU chain `28.018ms/tok`, agreement `16/16`. A rerun with pca-updown32 showed state-only draft improved versus lowrank (`31.190ms/tok` vs `38.806ms/tok` in that run) but was still slower than plain and free-run agreement collapsed to `1/16` for this early-layer route.
  verified_at: 2026-05-19

- claim: "Broad FFN deletion is not the speed lever for this route."
